# M26 — Tenant Billing: BR Collection, Self-Signup & Launch

**Phase:** Local Development
**Goal:** Turn the dark Billing foundation of M25 into a product Ikaro can charge for in Brazil: the **money loop** (issue the next charge, handle the provider webhook, sweep statuses, reconcile, self-heal, dunning, billing emails), everything a **manager** needs to see and do about their subscription (billing page, payment, identity, plan, cancel, reactivate, trial banner), **self-signup** with a free trial on the public `/ikaro-trial` page, the `TRIAL_SIGNUP_CTA` hotsite module, the **real Asaas adapter** with its nightly sandbox contract job, alerts/runbooks, and the launch-readiness story. The whole loop is built and tested against the `FakeBillingProvider` from M25 first; the real provider is the last backend story, so nothing before it needs a provider account.
**Depends on:** M25 (the `billing` schema and first-schema tables, `Subscription` / `BillingAccount` / `FeaturePrice` aggregates, `TenantAccessPort`, `IClock`, `BillingProviderPort` + `FakeBillingProvider` + contract-suite skeleton, the operator touchpoints, the suspension / hotsite / notification enforcement, the `exempt` backfill). M26 never redoes any of it; every M25 story it relies on is named in that story's `Dependencies`.
**Blocks:** M27 (purge / discard / deactivation), M28 (Stripe / US), M29 (automatic collection), M30 (annual billing) — reserved, drafted when their gates open (see `## Reserved follow-ups`).
**Design rationale:** `docs/discovery/tenant-billing/tenant-billing.md` (promoted via `/discovery-to-milestone` on 2026-10-29) — kept as the permanent *why*; this file and the canonical docs it cites (`docs/USE_CASES.md` UC-110–145, `docs/DOMAIN_MODEL.md` § Context 7: Billing Context, `docs/DOMAIN_EVENTS.md` § Billing Context Events, `docs/DATABASE_SCHEMA.md` § Schema: `billing`, `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` § Billing — …, `docs/LOCK_CATALOG.md` § Billing locks, `docs/RUNBOOKS.md` § Billing (planned), `docs/OBSERVABILITY_STRATEGY.md` § 7, `journeys/manager/billing.md`, `journeys/guest/ikaro-trial.md`) are the implementation source. Where a canonical doc and the discovery doc differ, the canonical doc wins.

---

## Follow-up milestones to write (reminders — nothing here is drafted yet)

M27–M30 have numbers and outlines (§ Reserved follow-ups below) but **no milestone files and no stories**. Draft each one with the same milestone-drafting steps used for M25/M26 (group its UCs into stories, sequence the waves, write the stories, audit), working from the already-promoted canonical docs — no new `docs/discovery/` folder is needed unless the "Needs first" column says so.

| Milestone | Write it when | Needs first |
|---|---|---|
| **M27** purge / discard / deactivation (UC-132, 141, 142) | **Before M26 launches** (a launch condition: M26-S39). The first possible erasure is 90 days after the first real suspension, which can only happen after launch. | A short discovery for the per-context deletion rules (what each of the nine contexts erases, what it keeps for fiscal reasons) and for who emits `TenantDeactivated`; the M17-S51 data inventory feeds it. |
| **M29** Pix Automático + hosted card (UC-135, 143) | When the real Asaas production PJ account is live (M26-S39 observed) | The end-to-end Pix Automático verification in sandbox and production; for card, the spike's F2 checks (M25-S01). No new discovery. |
| **M30** annual billing (UC-144, 145) | When the owner sets the annual prices | The annual price list. No new discovery. |
| **M28** Stripe / US (no UC of its own) | When a Stripe account exists and the accountant has decided US sales-tax handling | The accountant's decision and real Stripe fees; a short discovery only if the tax decision changes how tax lines work. |

Until M27 is written, `summary.canDiscard` stays `false` and the `billing-tenant-purge` cron stays disabled (see Non-Goals).

---

## Non-Goals

- **Purge, discard and deactivation (UC-132, UC-141, UC-142).** M27. The `billing.tenant_purge_*` tables already exist (M25-S03) but no M26 story reads or writes them. Consequences that M26 states rather than hides: `summary.canDiscard` is always `false` in M26 (no purge participant has shipped), UC-127 A3's second action is replaced by the honest "contact Ikaro" message, and the `purgeAt` clean-up date is shown on the suspended page labelled as the **earliest** possible date, but no job acts on it. The `billing-tenant-purge` trigger is **not** registered.
- **Automatic collection (UC-135 Pix Automático, UC-143 hosted card), annual billing (UC-144, UC-145), Stripe / US (no UC of its own).** M28–M30. The "Cobrança automática" tab renders the "Em breve" state (UC-117) with no endpoint behind it; `cycle` is always `MONTHLY`; the BR billing flow is tax-inclusive (`taxMinor = 0`, `taxLines = []`); the US option is not offered (UC-127 A7: no Stripe adapter before M28; the US price rows exist in every environment since M25-S04, so this story's signup must gate the country explicitly, not by the absence of prices). No `charge_tax_lines`, no `next_cycle` handling, no `origin = PROVIDER_SUBSCRIPTION` charges are written.
- **UC-129 (first-run onboarding checklist).** Delivered only if M24's onboarding wizard (UC-075) ships; an M24 follow-up, not a story here.
- **Self-service data export and the per-context deletion rules of the purge pipeline.** No use case yet (M27 outline only).
- **Changing the commercial catalog.** The offered features, tiers, prices, chatbot caps and the Plus model were settled in M25-S02; M26 consumes them (S21 maps tier → model and caps in code).
- **A manual "mark paid" route or any operator payment override.** None exists by design (`docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` § Provider port): a payment the provider confirmed but Ikaro missed is repaired by reconcile.
- **Promoted-but-unused concepts (milestone check).** The `charge_tax_lines` / `next_cycle` / `ADDON` / `origin` pieces of the first schema and `startAutoBilling` / `revokeAutoBilling` on `BillingProviderPort` are promoted for fidelity of the first schema and the port, built by no M26 story (beyond the typed `NotSupported` stubs S37 gives the Asaas adapter), and owned by M28–M30. `tenant_purge_jobs` / `tenant_purge_steps` are owned by M27.

## Known open items

- **Gates the owner must open (not code):** (1) the Asaas **production** PJ account (KYC lead time unknown) and the NFS-e configuration in the Asaas account; (2) the accountant's confirmation that provider-automatic NFS-e satisfies the Brazilian duty; (3) the **Terms of Use and Privacy Policy** texts and their versions (UC-127 step 3, `acceptedTermsVersion`), supplied by the owner / legal — M26-S33 hosts them but does not write them; (4) the Asaas facts that M25-S01's sandbox spike **cannot** observe: M25-S01 checks 10–14 already recorded `invoiceUrl` validity, the webhook payload fields, `externalReference` length, `notificationDisabled` and the minimum charge; what remains for the real account is the event that finalizes a **lost** chargeback, the callback-domain rule behind `returnUrl`, and the behaviour of the production account (whether `notificationDisabled` also silences the payer's NFS-e email, the contracted fee table; `docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md` § 4). S37 checks what the sandbox can show against the real adapter and S39 closes the rest with a production smoke charge; the webhook-queue re-enable was in scope of M25-S01 check 5 and is only re-confirmed there. Stories S37 and S39 are marked **GATE**.
- **Trigger names (settled, M26 decision):** the six cron trigger constants are `cron-billing-issue-charges`, `cron-billing-status-sweep`, `cron-billing-reminders`, `cron-billing-reconcile`, `cron-billing-self-heal` and `cron-billing-provider-events-purge` (precedent: `CRON_LEAD_FORM_RETENTION_TRIGGER = 'cron-lead-form-retention'`). The constant is also the literal Pub/Sub topic (`ikaro-${name}`), the Cloud Scheduler job is `ikaro-` + the topic key (`ikaro-cron-billing-issue-charges`, as `docs/RUNBOOKS.md` already states) and the manual publisher is `POST /cron/billing-…`. `modules/scheduler/variables.tf` rejects any `cron-*` topic without a `locals.jobs` entry, so each trigger story adds its job in the same PR; the canonical docs' "Trigger name" column (`docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` § Billing cron triggers, `docs/USE_CASES.md`) is corrected to the `cron-billing-*` names by the docs owner.
- **HTTP statuses "decided at story discovery":** `BILLING_CHARGE_NOT_PAYABLE` and which charge statuses count as not payable (S15); the rejections of `PUT /v1/billing/plan` (`BILLING_FEATURE_DEPENDENCY_UNMET`, `…_HAS_DEPENDENTS`, `…_IN_USE`, `…_PLAN_EMPTY`, `…_PLAN_CHANGE_NOT_ALLOWED`, `…_NO_PRICE_FOR_COUNTRY`, `…_ADDRESS_REQUIRED`) (S19); the status of `GET /v1/public/signup/session` without a valid cookie, of `resend` when the first manager already exists, and the public mapping of `BILLING_NO_PRICE_FOR_COUNTRY` (S24, S25); the error code and `violations[]` shape for a non-owner using `TRIAL_SIGNUP_CTA` (S26). Each is flagged in the story that implements it, never guessed.
- **`SubscriptionBanner` mount point (S32):** `apps/web/app/dashboard/layout.tsx` is a bare passthrough (`docs/DASHBOARD_FRONTEND_ARCHITECTURE.md` § Staff/Manager Shell), so the banner mounts in `shells/dashboard/components/DashboardShell.tsx`, the one component the ten section layouts render (S32 verifies this and tests the banner on every one of the ten sections).
- **Production before the Asaas KYC (decided, M26-D11):** two env feature flags (repo convention, no external system) let M26 code ship to production before a live provider account exists. `FEATURE_FLAG_BILLING_PROVIDER_LIVE` (default `false` in production until S39; `true` in local, CI and staging) gates the live adapter: while `false` the boot does not require `ASAAS_API_KEY` / `ASAAS_WEBHOOK_TOKEN`, the provider registry refuses live merchant keys, and the webhook route answers exactly `200` as a logged no-op. `FEATURE_FLAG_SIGNUP_ENABLED` (default `false` in production until S39; `true` elsewhere) gates self-signup: while `false` the signup routes answer `503 SIGNUP_CLOSED`, `/ikaro-trial` shows a "cadastro indisponível" notice, and slug availability and prices stay readable. It is also the real signup rollback lever drilled in S39 (disabling the `TRIAL_SIGNUP_CTA` module is not a kill switch). S01 declares the first flag (`FEATURE_FLAG_BILLING_PROVIDER_LIVE`), S24 declares the second (`FEATURE_FLAG_SIGNUP_ENABLED`).
- **Staging webhook reachability (decided):** the staging BFF has `INGRESS_TRAFFIC_INTERNAL_ONLY` and no public invoker (TD38), so Asaas cannot call it there. Staging is verified with the fake's `simulate` route and recorded payloads (the nightly contract run, S37); real Asaas → BFF reachability is proven in S39 by the production smoke charge, before `FEATURE_FLAG_SIGNUP_ENABLED` is turned on.
- **Known limitations stated rather than hidden:** "3 business days" in the settlement margin means Monday–Friday with no holiday calendar in M26; reminders match the exact `daysOffset`, so a cron day that did not run loses that milestone; a self-heal-created trial for a tenant that was meant to be `exempt` is a paying trial (the exempt intent lives only on the `SignupRegistration`, which self-heal cannot locate from the tenant alone, and `TenantProvisioned` carries no hint; the operator fixes it with UC-137, S08).
- **Audit decisions to re-confirm at the owning story's `/story-discovery` (set by the 2026-11 docs-audit fix; none is locked by the owner yet):** (1) a full refund of the first invoice while `TRIALING` leaves the charge `REFUNDED`, treated as unpaid at trial end so the recovery path applies — the alternative is making `UQ_billing_charges_period` ignore `REFUNDED` so a replacement can be issued (S04, S06, S36; `docs/DOMAIN_MODEL.md`, `docs/RUNBOOKS.md` follow S04); (2) the signup resend keeps a 60 s button cooldown that is cosmetic only — the enforced limits are 3/h per tenant and 10/day per IP (S25, S33); (3) the HTTP statuses of `BILLING_CANCEL_NOT_ALLOWED`, `BILLING_REACTIVATE_NOT_ALLOWED`, `BILLING_PRICE_CURRENCY_MISMATCH`, `PLATFORM_HOTSITE_MODULE_OWNER_ONLY` and the M26 use of `BILLING_CYCLE_NOT_AVAILABLE` (S20, S26, S38); (4) `POST /internal/signup/validate` and the `POST /internal/staff/resend-first-manager-invitation` shapes, the signup cookie names and `POST /auth/dev-signup-session` (S24, S25); (5) B-P1 and `PRICE_ANNOUNCE_MAX_TENANTS = 5000` (S38); (6) S36 and S37 staging checks must not run at the same time (the S36 live check pauses the staging reconcile job); (7) the S24 tax-id half of the one-trial rule is best-effort under concurrency, and an early `ExtendGrace` reuses `BILLING_GRACE_NOT_ALLOWED`; (8) the Charge-derived fields of the operator tenants list moved to S14. If discovery changes one, update this plan and the canonical docs together.
- **Other story-discovery items:** the `/ikaro-trial` rewrite mechanism and its CSP allowance (S24, S33), the internal route that carries Platform's signup use case from the BFF (S24), and the `Money` / `MinorAmount` reuse (settled in M25-S04).
- **M24 interaction (recorded, built here):** the BOOKING-removal guard (S18) must also count M24's class-session bookings, enrollments and waitlist entries when those exist; the story states it as an acceptance criterion with a "recount when M24 lands" note. M24's own routes are annotated by whoever lands them after the M25 detector flip (`plan/M24-…` § Billing interaction).
- **M17-S51 (recorded, not a story):** the manual offboarding runbook stays the interim path for erasing a never-paid tenant (UC-127 A3 message, `docs/RUNBOOKS.md` § Billing "Interim" entry) until M27.
- **Percentage-adjust and exact price changes (S38)** may follow launch; the launch does not depend on them (prices are seeded in M25-S04). Listed in the last wave on purpose.

Wave notes (a wave is a sequencing guarantee, not a concurrency one — see `## Run-batch preview`): **Wave 1** is backend only and runs entirely on the fake provider; there is no Wave 0 in M26 because M25 already owns the migration / backfill safety (the schema and the `exempt` backfill exist before any M26 code runs, and M26 adds **no breaking migration** — one expand-only index on `platform.tenants` (S08, `AddTenantsCreatedAtIndex`) and one data seed (S11, the notification templates)). **Wave 2** is backend / BFF for the manager and starts after the charge model (S02). **Wave 3** is backend / BFF / packages for signup and the hotsite module contract. **Wave 4** is frontend only and starts after the backend stories it reads from. **Wave 5** is the E2E, the observability / runbook work, the real adapter and launch readiness — the real adapter (S37) is deliberately last among the backend stories: everything before it is proven against the fake, and the nightly contract job then proves the fake and Asaas agree.

---

## Design & test strategy (milestone-level)

**Shared patterns (decided once; every story cites them).**
- **M26-D1 — One use case, three entry points.** Applying a provider fact to a charge and its subscription is a single application service (`ApplyChargeFactUseCase`, introduced by S04) called by the webhook (UC-114), by reconcile (UC-116) and by the on-demand refresh behind `payment-return` (UC-119). The paid-state transition, the reactivation of a `PAST_DUE` / `SUSPENDED` subscription and the recovery-charge settlement therefore exist **once**; S07 and S15 add entry points, never a second copy of the rules.
- **M26-D2 — `Charge` is its own aggregate; paying it touches two aggregates in one use case.** `Charge` (S02) is not a child of `Subscription` (one per period forever). `ApplyChargeFactUseCase` loads the subscription with `findByTenantIdForUpdate` (lock **B-R1**, `docs/LOCK_CATALOG.md` § Billing locks) first, then writes the charge through `chargeRepo.save()`; the order is subscription → charge rows, never the reverse, in every M26 use case (S04, S05, S06, S07, S15, S19, S20 — S07 and S15 through `ApplyChargeFactUseCase`). Status is recomputable, so the invariant never rests on the lock alone (`Subscription.version` is the second line).
- **M26-D3 — Provider I/O is never inside `txManager.run()`.** Charge creation is two steps: insert the `Charge` + lines (`provider_creation_status = PENDING`) and advance `next_due_date` in one transaction; call the provider **after** commit; store `READY` (id, URL, expiry) in a second short transaction; publish `ChargeIssued` only after `READY` (S05). The same shape applies to cancelling a stale charge at the provider (S06, S19, S20) and to creating the recovery charge at the provider (S06, S20): the local state is saved first (a `PENDING` recovery charge), the provider call follows after commit, `READY` is stored in a second short transaction; the provider side of a cancel is recorded in the new `billing.charges.provider_cancel_pending` flag (set in the local transaction, cleared after the provider confirms) and retried by reconcile (S07), and a `PENDING` recovery charge by S05's retry. **Single creator per charge:** before any `createCharge` (issue job, retry, recovery creation) the creator claims the charge in a short transaction (`provider_creation_last_attempt_at` null or older than 10 minutes, `provider_creation_attempts + 1`, `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`) and looks the charge up at the provider by `externalReference` (our charge id) before creating, so two concurrent creators (a manual `POST /cron/…` plus the scheduled run, two instances, the retry versus the sweep's own second step) produce one provider charge — Asaas has no idempotency key. CI enforces the no-I/O-in-transaction rule (`transactional-io`) (`transactional-io`); S04, S05, S06, S07, S15, S19 and S20 each carry the acceptance criterion.
- **M26-D4 — Idempotency lives in the database.** `UNIQUE(tenant_id, subscription_id, period_start)` (ignoring cancelled charges) makes a double-run issue job a no-op; `provider_events(provider, event_id)` makes a replayed webhook a `200` no-op; the unique constraint on open recovery charges (`UQ_billing_charges_open_recovery`, M25-S03 — catalogued in `docs/LOCK_CATALOG.md`; it is **not** the lock-catalog id B-Q1, which is the purge-job unique owned by M27) makes a double suspension or double reactivate return the same recovery charge. Every job and handler states its redelivery behaviour as an acceptance criterion.
- **M26-D5 — The seven-trigger family.** Six cron triggers are built in M26 (issue, sweep, reminders, reconcile, self-heal, provider-events purge); the seventh (tenant purge) is M27. Each is registered with `ITriggerBus.registerTrigger(name, handler, consumer)`, passes an explicit `tenantId` to every use case (no `RequestContext`), skips `exempt` subscriptions, does **not** use the shared `TenantScopedJob` helper (`docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_BACKEND.md` § Billing (planned): Billing's jobs deliberately select their own rows — there is no M25-S22 dependency and no `includeSuspended` switch), selects its rows through its own **standalone partial index** (created in M25-S03; each job story re-checks the index covers its filter, queries it through a `…ForJob` selector on the repository port, and compares tenant-local dates by selecting on UTC with ±1 day slack and then checking the local date per subscription) and applies no operational-status filter, so the sweep, reconcile, reminders and self-heal reach `SUSPENDED` / non-operational tenants (a `SUSPENDED` tenant with an open recovery charge is exactly what reconcile and the reminders serve), and has a thin manual publisher `POST /cron/billing-…` behind `InternalApiGuard` (`CronLeadFormController` is the precedent). Each trigger story owns its devops half (scheduler job, topic, subscription) with the playbook row and a live-verification check, and also updates the hard-coded scheduler test fixture (`infra/terraform/modules/scheduler/tests/resources_unit_test.tftest.hcl` lists every cron topic and asserts the job count) — S05 introduces the per-job `paused` switch (production jobs are created paused, S39 enables them). The constants are `cron-billing-*` (Known open items). **Every new route** (backend and BFF: webhook, cron, internal, manager billing routes) carries `@NoFeatureGate()` plus an explicit `@SuspensionAccess(...)` (M25-S16 / S20 / S24: the route-completeness detector is enforcing), and every Billing `subscribe()` passes `skipWhenSuspended: false` (M25-S21b).
- **M26-D6 — Emails are event handlers.** Billing never calls Notification; domain events (S03) feed handlers (S12) that call one send use case each, using platform-level templates (S11). Billing templates are exempt from the suspension gate (M25-S23).
- **M26-D7 — BR is tax-inclusive.** `taxMinor` is `0` and `taxLines` is `[]` for every M26 charge; the field shapes exist so M28 does not change the contract.
- **M26-D8 — Money is integer minor units + ISO currency** (M25-D4); the Billing-local `MinorAmount` VO from M25-S04 is used everywhere.
- **M26-D9 — Everything is proven on the fake first.** The contract suite from M25-S14 runs on the fake on every PR; S37 runs the same suite against the Asaas sandbox and adds the nightly job. A scenario the fake can produce (Pix `RECEIVED` only; boleto `CONFIRMED` → `RECEIVED`; card `CONFIRMED` then `RECEIVED` ~32 days later; duplicates, delays, out-of-order, provider 5xx) is a named integration test in the story that owns the behaviour.
- **M26-D10 — One provider-port method list.** `BillingProviderPort` is the M25-S14 port, and its method list is **complete from M25-S14**: `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` (§ Provider port) already lists every method (`ensureCustomer`, `createCharge` with its `returnUrl` input, `regeneratePaymentLink`, `cancelCharge`, `startAutoBilling`, `revokeAutoBilling`, `fetchChargeStatus`, `readAccountHealth`, `reenableWebhookQueue`), and M25-S14 promotes `parseWebhook` into the interface block. M26 therefore adds **no method to the port and no method list to the doc**: S05 only *uses* the `returnUrl` input of `createCharge` (S28 reads it; S37 maps it to Asaas's `callback` only if Asaas's callback-domain rule allows it), and S37 adds the typed `NotSupported` stubs for `startAutoBilling` / `revokeAutoBilling` to the Asaas adapter (M29 builds them). A **typed error is not a method**: the port and the fake raise a typed `ChargeAlreadyPaidError` from `cancelCharge` when the provider refuses to cancel a charge that was already paid (the signal S06 / S19 / S20 route through `ApplyChargeFactUseCase`), with a contract case; M25-S14's text carries it. Every M26 use case obtains the adapter from `BillingProviderRegistry.resolve(merchantProfileKey)` (M25-S14), never from a bare port token. If a story finds the built port differs from the doc, the doc is corrected in that story's change.

**Aggregates and boundaries (named pass).**
- `Charge` (root): kind (`PERIOD` | `RECOVERY`; `ADDON` is M30), period dates (null forever for `RECOVERY`), due date (tenant-timezone date), immutable `ChargeLine` snapshot (feature, tier, unit price), subtotal / amount, status (`PENDING | PAID | OVERDUE | FAILED | CANCELED | REFUNDED | CHARGEBACK`), method, `paidAt`, provider ids and URL, `paymentReturnSeenAt`, `refundedAmountMinor`, `providerCreationStatus`. Invariants in the aggregate: legal status transitions (a paid charge can only become `REFUNDED` / `CHARGEBACK`; a cancelled one never becomes paid unless the provider proves it was paid), immutability of the lines and amounts after insert, only status / payment fields written afterwards.
- `Subscription` / `BillingAccount` change only through methods defined in M25-S08 plus the payment-driven transitions M26 adds, each owned by the **first** story that needs it (one name everywhere): **S04** `markPeriodPaid`, `reactivateOnPayment`, `suspend(reason)` (incl. `PAYMENT_REVERSED`) and the `BillingAccount` cross-tenant lookup by provider customer id; **S05** the `BillingAccount.provider_customer_id` setter; **S06** `markPastDue`, `activateAfterTrial`; **S19** `scheduleItems`, `confirmPlan`; **S20** `scheduleCancel`, `clearCancel`. Each new method gets a unit test next to the M25 ones; M25-S08 states that these are M26's.
- `ProviderEvent` is an inbox record per `docs/DATABASE_SCHEMA.md` § `billing.provider_events` (PK `(provider, event_id)`, `tenant_id` NOT NULL, `event_type`, `received_at`, `processed_at`; **no payload column**), not an aggregate; `SignupRegistration` is the M25-S09 thin record.
- **Cross-aggregate check:** the only pair that must stay consistent in one transaction is `Charge` + `Subscription` (payment, recovery, cancel-and-reissue); M26-D2 states the order and the lock. No other M26 story needs two aggregates consistent.
- **Unused-promotion check:** `ProviderEvent` and `Charge` are owned here; the tax / cycle / purge tables are not (Non-Goals).

**Locks and constraints (all in `docs/LOCK_CATALOG.md`; each needs a named test).**
- **B-R1** (S04, S05, S06, S07, S15, S19, S20; S07 and S15 take it through `ApplyChargeFactUseCase`): *concurrency test — a webhook confirming a payment racing the status sweep on the same subscription serialises and the paid state wins* (S06 owns it; S04 owns "two webhooks for one charge"); a manager's cancel racing a webhook (S20).
- **`UQ_billing_charges_open_recovery`** (S02 repository, S06, S20): *concurrency test — two simultaneous suspensions / reactivations produce exactly one open recovery charge.*
- **B-P1** (S38): the price-batch apply row lock `findByCountryForUpdate`, taken `ORDER BY id` and never combined with B-R1 in one transaction.
- **B-A1** (M25-S09) is exercised end to end by S24: *concurrency test — two simultaneous self-signups for one Google account produce exactly one trial.*
- Every lock port method added by an M26 story is catalogued in `docs/LOCK_CATALOG.md` (CI: `lock-catalog-coverage`); the story that implements a lock or unique **names its concurrency test next to the catalog row** (`docs/LOCK_CATALOG.md` checklist) — S02, S04, S06, S19, S20 and S24 therefore list `docs/LOCK_CATALOG.md` in Files.

**Migration ordering.** M26 adds **no breaking migration**: one expand-only index on `platform.tenants` (S08, `AddTenantsCreatedAtIndex`, a plain `CREATE INDEX` — not `CONCURRENTLY`, because `migrationsTransactionMode` is `all`, see `1748400000013-AddStartedAtIndexToChatbotSessions` and `1748500000009-AddResourceIdToScheduleClosuresAndOpenings` — no column or constraint change) and one data seed — S11's notification template seed (platform-scoped rows with `tenant_id IS NULL`, both languages, kept out of the per-tenant default copy by the key set described in S11) and nothing else; `charges`, `charge_lines` and `provider_events` already exist from M25-S03. Code that reads `charges` ships after S02. The Notification change in S11 is an additive lookup — the existing tenant-strict lookup is untouched.

**Cross-context edges (milestone-level).** M26 adds exactly: one exact-path `permittedEdges` entry for Billing → Booking (S18) and, in Notification, nine exact-path entries for the handlers importing Billing event / command classes (S12); the Billing → Platform tenant-info edge from M25-S13 only gains one target path (S08). Everything else is an event, a BFF call or an existing port.

**Performance.** (1) The hourly sweep, the daily issue / reminders jobs, the hourly reconcile (with a daily full pass) and self-heal are **cross-tenant, unscoped system jobs**: each selects through the standalone partial index named in `docs/DATABASE_SCHEMA.md` § `billing.subscriptions` / `billing.charges` (`next_due_date`, open-charge status, `suspended_at`, `trial_ends_at`), batches its work, processes one subscription per transaction, and is bounded by a per-run limit so one slow provider call cannot starve the rest (CLAUDE.md §7 "cross-tenant, unscoped system job"; `EXPLAIN` asserted in each job's integration test). (2) The charge history is paginated by `(tenant_id, due_date DESC)`. (3) The webhook route is hot under a provider retry storm: it does one indexed insert (the inbox), one indexed charge lookup and one subscription row lock; it is `@SkipThrottle()` and returns `200` after commit. (4) The public price and slug routes are cacheable / throttled (S22, S23) so the public page cannot become a database hit per visitor.

**Backward compatibility.** M26 changes existing behaviour in three places only: Platform's `ProvisionTenantUseCase` is *called by* the new signup use case (it is not modified here — its M25-S10 changes already shipped), the hotsite module registry gains a 13th type (S26 — existing tenants, templates and the editor are unaffected because the type is owner-only and absent from templates), and Booking gains a read use case plus a `bookableUntil` input honoured by every window consumer (S18). **Non-regression contract, owned by S18:** with no `bookableUntil` set (every tenant today), every booking-window consumer returns exactly what it returned before — asserted by running the existing window / availability / recurring / alert integration suites unchanged plus a new table-driven test that a `null` cap equals today's result.

**Test pyramid.** Unit (`.spec.ts`) for the aggregate, VOs, use cases, handlers, mappers; integration (`.integration.spec.ts`) per use case with tenant-isolation scenarios and the named concurrency tests; the contract suite on the fake every PR and on the Asaas sandbox nightly (S37); E2E (Playwright) only in the frontend stories and the cross-cutting S35 (money loop on the fake, per-role suspended experience). The mocked-vs-real rule: provider behaviour is never `jest.fn()`-stubbed — the fake adapter is used (`no-jest-fn-for-repository-or-port`).

**Spanning scenario (golden path), implemented by S35 (Playwright) with backend integration slices in S04–S06 and S24:**
visitor opens `/ikaro-trial` (S33), signs up with Google, tax id and terms (S24) → `TenantProvisioned` creates a `TRIALING` subscription (M25-S11) and the first-manager invitation → the manager signs in and sees the **trial banner** (S32) and the Resumo (S27) → confirms the plan (S30/S19) → `simulate` advances time (S13 + M25-S12) to ten days before trial end → the **issue job** creates the first charge (S05) → the manager opens "Pagar agora" (S28/S15) → `simulate` delivers a Pix `PAID` event (S13/S04) → the webhook marks the charge paid and the page leaves "pagamento recebido, reativando…" (S27) → time advances past trial end → the **sweep** turns the subscription `ACTIVE` (S06) → a later charge goes unpaid → `PAST_DUE` banner (S32) → past 14 days the sweep suspends and issues the recovery charge → the manager lands on the billing page with "Pague 1 mês para reativar" (S32/S27) → pays → `ACTIVE`. **Observation side confirmed:** every actor-visible state in this scenario has a supplying read — status / dates / open charge / history (S14), payment pending flag (S14 + S15), banner states (S14 `summary` fields), suspended page + recovery charge (S14), plan state (S17), signup eligibility and account-created state (S24), operator investigation list (M25-S15b).

**Interim / infra.** One shared devops story for provider secrets and the webhook env (S01); every cron, event-topic or new-subscription story owns its devops half with the playbook row from `infra/terraform/README.md` § New-resource PR-sequencing playbook and a named live-verification check (CLAUDE.md §9); alert policies and KPIs in S36; the nightly sandbox workflow in S37. Terraform / IAM / Pub/Sub / CI-CD stories are never presented merge-ready without their live check.

## Reserved follow-ups (not drafted until their gates open)

- **M27 — Purge, discard, deactivation** (UC-132, UC-141, UC-142). *Gate:* the first possible erasure is 90 days after the first suspension, so M27 must ship before the first suspended tenant ages out; the `billing-tenant-purge` cron stays disabled until all nine purge participants (storage, provider cancellation, Billing anonymization, the seven bounded-context deletions) ship, and `summary.canDiscard` flips on only then. Open there: the per-context deletion rules, the emitter of `TenantDeactivated` (sequenced with M17-S51), the self-service data export (no UC yet).
- **M28 — Stripe / US** (no UC of its own; cross-cuts UC-113, 114, 118, 127, 128). *Gate:* a Stripe account, the accountant's US sales-tax decision and real Stripe fees; adds `charge_tax_lines`, `StripeBillingProvider`, and the US price list. US identity (M25-S06 added the `EIN` type): validate the 9-digit EIN, which is shape-checked only today, and send it to Stripe as `us_ein`; widen the `tax_id_type` CHECK only if M28 needs a new type.
  - **Stripe discovery and fake update (carried from M25-S14, decided 2026-11-11).** `FakeBillingProvider` is Asaas-shaped and serves `BRL_FAKE` only; `USD_FAKE` throws `ProviderNotSupportedError` because no Stripe behaviour has been observed. M28 therefore starts with a Stripe discovery that produces a canonical Stripe provider doc under `docs/` (named `STRIPE_PROVIDER`, not yet created), the same shape as `docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md` (observed in Stripe test mode, labelled V / O / I). Then, in this order: (1) extend the contract suite (`apps/backend/src/test/contracts/billing-provider.contract.ts`) first, with the Stripe-specific cases (signature verification inside `parseWebhook`, the `automatic_tax` breakdown in `createCharge`, finalized-invoice event sequences); (2) make the fake Stripe-faithful and register `USD_FAKE`; (3) add `StripeBillingProvider` and append its path to the `externalSideEffectPorts` entries; (4) add `USD_STRIPE_TEST` and run the same suite against Stripe test mode in the nightly job; (5) revisit whether `PaymentChargeback` and `NormalizedChargeStatus` need Stripe-specific fields.
  - **US tenants before M28.** `resolveMerchantProfileKey` already gives a US tenant `USD_FAKE` locally and `USD_STRIPE_LIVE` in production, and neither has an adapter until M28. Every M26 caller of `BillingProviderRegistry` (the issue-charges job, status sweep, reconcile, self-heal) must treat `ProviderNotSupportedError` for such a subscription as skip-and-log, never a crash; the plan carries no such line yet, so whichever M26 story first resolves a provider owns it.
- **M29 — Automatic collection** (UC-135 Pix Automático, UC-143 hosted recurring card). *Gate:* the real PJ account with a CNPJ active for 6+ months and the end-to-end Pix Automático verification in sandbox and production; F2 also needs the spike's F2 checks.
- **M30 — Annual billing** (UC-144, UC-145). *Gate:* the owner sets the annual prices; adds `cycle`, `next_cycle*`, the co-terminus `ADDON` charge and the 30-day annual issue lead.
- Numbers M27–M30 are reserved; M115 is an existing completed milestone, so M27–M30 do not collide with it.
- **Slice letters in the canonical docs** (`docs/API_CONTRACTS.md`, `docs/DATA_RETENTION_AND_PRIVACY.md`, the discovery docs) are not in milestone-number order: M28 Stripe / US = slice E, M29 automatic collection = slice F (F1 Pix Automático, F2 hosted card), M27 purge / discard = slice G, M30 annual = slice H.

## Wave 1 — Money loop on the fake provider (backend)

### M26-S01 — Provider secrets, the live-provider flag and the webhook env contract

**Agent:** `devops + backend-ts`
**Complexity:** M
**Docs to load:** `infra/terraform/README.md` § New-resource PR-sequencing playbook (the "new secret" rows and the three-places IAM rule) and § Gotchas, `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_INFRA.md` § Billing configuration (planned) — live adapters, fake/sandbox keys and the scheduler jobs, `infra/terraform/SECRETS.md`, `docs/USE_CASES.md` UC-114 (preconditions), `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` § Billing — Provider Webhooks and Internal API (authenticity)
**Dependencies:** M25-S07 (the billing env contract already carries the owner slug), M25-S01 (the sandbox spike confirms the secret names and the webhook token mechanism), M25-S14 (the provider registry and its live-key guard, which this story's flag feeds)
**Pattern:** plain composition — Secret Manager containers wired through the existing `secret_env_vars` and the env contract, plus one plain feature flag; no new module.

**Description:**
Create the provider secrets and the environment variables the money loop and the real adapter need, in the order the Terraform playbook requires, so no later story is blocked on an infra side-effect.

**Secrets (one container per secret in each environment's own GCP project; the staging project holds the sandbox values, the production project the live values — never both in one project):** `asaas-api-key` (consumed by `cloudrun_backend` as `ASAAS_API_KEY`: needed in **staging too**, because `BRL_ASAAS_SANDBOX` calls the sandbox) and `asaas-webhook-token` (consumed by `cloudrun_bff` as `ASAAS_WEBHOOK_TOKEN`). **The webhook token is a BFF-only secret:** the BFF compares the `asaas-access-token` header timing-safe and the backend's internal route is protected by `InternalApiGuard`; the backend never receives or verifies the token and `parseWebhook` only normalises (authenticity is a BFF test, S04). The `merchant_profile_key` is **not** a secret and not an env var — it is data on the subscription, chosen per environment (`BRL_FAKE` local / CI, `BRL_ASAAS_SANDBOX` staging, the live key in production; the names come from M25-S07's `resolveMerchantProfileKey` and the guard of M25-S14). The Asaas base URL is a constant of the adapter selected by `APP_ENV` (decided with M25-S14's guard), not an env var. `TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY` (secret `turnstile-secret-key`) is not new either (the lead form has it). No `STRIPE_*` secret is created here (M28).

**Env contract.** Added to `apps/backend/src/config/env.validation.ts` (`ASAAS_API_KEY`, `FEATURE_FLAG_BILLING_PROVIDER_LIVE`), `apps/bff/src/config/env.validation.ts` (`ASAAS_WEBHOOK_TOKEN`, `FEATURE_FLAG_BILLING_PROVIDER_LIVE` — the webhook route is a no-op while it is off) and `packages/infra-scripts/src/env-contract.ts`. **`FEATURE_FLAG_BILLING_PROVIDER_LIVE`** (boolean; default `false` when `APP_ENV=production`, `true` otherwise; Terraform sets it explicitly to `false` in `envs/prod` and `true` in `envs/staging` as a plain `env_vars` entry, so S39 turns production on by a one-line change): while `false` the boot does **not** require `ASAAS_API_KEY` / `ASAAS_WEBHOOK_TOKEN`, the provider registry (M25-S14) refuses live merchant profile keys, and the BFF webhook route answers exactly `200` as a logged no-op (S04); when `true` the two secrets are required (a `refine` on the schema, so a `true` flag without the secrets fails startup). `ASAAS_API_KEY` and `ASAAS_WEBHOOK_TOKEN` are listed in the relevant `exemptKeys` (local and CI have no Asaas credentials; they are still wired in both Terraform roots). **The refusal of a `*_FAKE` profile key paired with a live adapter is the M25-S14 registry guard, not `env.validation.ts`** (the key is a database column that the env schema cannot see); this story only feeds it the flag. The docs owner adds the flag to `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_INFRA.md` § Billing configuration (planned).

**Pre-launch, no real traffic depends on the next deploy succeeding, so the two-PR secret row applies — provided this PR creates no new Pub/Sub subscription (rule 3).** PR1 creates the containers (`modules/secrets` `base_secret_ids`), wires `secret_env_vars` and the plain `env_vars` flag, and adds the schema entries together; **as soon as PR1 merges, populate the sandbox values in the staging project** (the playbook's ordered step; the production values are populated by S39, not here); PR2 is the `foundation`-only accessor-grant PR (see Files: the grant lives in **three places** — `runtime-identities`' `secret_accessors_base` lists, both Foundation env roots' `secret_ids` maps, and the module test fixtures). Between PR1 and PR2 the next `envs/*` revision for the affected service fails resolving the secret and the previous revision keeps serving; the stale-revision gotcha's forced redeploy is the recovery. No scheduler job or topic is added in this story — each trigger story owns its own. **Webhook-token rotation (documented order, no dual-token machinery):** the BFF compares against the single `ASAAS_WEBHOOK_TOKEN`; to rotate, write the new secret version and redeploy the BFF, then change the token in the Asaas account immediately after the new revision is serving. Deliveries between the two steps get `401` and are retried by Asaas; anything missed is caught up by reconcile (S07), and a queue the provider paused in the gap is re-enabled by self-heal (S08). The runbook rotation entry (S36) states this order and the post-rotation check that the queue is not paused.

**Backend use case steps:** none.
**Backend HTTP surface:** none.
**New migration / i18n keys / env vars / feature flags:** secrets `asaas-api-key` and `asaas-webhook-token`; env vars `ASAAS_API_KEY` (backend), `ASAAS_WEBHOOK_TOKEN` (BFF), `FEATURE_FLAG_BILLING_PROVIDER_LIVE` (backend and BFF). `FEATURE_FLAG_SIGNUP_ENABLED` is declared and owned by S24, not S01.

**PR sequence:** 2 PRs — matches the playbook row "Same, but a temporary broken deploy is acceptable (pre-launch …) and this PR does not also create a new Pub/Sub subscription".
- PR1 (`infra-app-mix-ok`, because `apps/**` and `infra/terraform/**` change together): the secret containers, `secret_env_vars` and `env_vars` wiring, and the schema / env-contract entries, together (satisfies `env-contract` immediately). Then **populate the staging sandbox values** and confirm the service redeploys healthy once PR2 lands.
- PR2 (`foundation` only — the playbook forbids mixing `foundation/**` with any other `infra/terraform/**` path in one PR): the accessor grants for the backend service account on `asaas-api-key` and the BFF service account on `asaas-webhook-token`, then the Foundation apply.

**IAM / permissions:** the runtime service accounts read their own secret versions (`roles/secretmanager.secretAccessor` on the specific secrets): backend on `asaas-api-key`, BFF on `asaas-webhook-token` — same pattern as the existing secrets; no new principal.
**Live-verification check:** on staging after PR2: (1) `gcloud secrets versions access latest --secret=asaas-webhook-token` returns a value with the operator's own identity (never pasted in a PR or log); (2) `gcloud run services describe <backend|bff> --format='value(spec.template.spec.containers[0].env)'` shows the new var names bound to secrets and the flag value; (3) a fresh revision starts and `/health/ready` is 200 (a missing secret fails the revision, not silently); (4) the ingress and `vpc_egress` check required by `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_INFRA.md` § Billing configuration (planned), **per environment**: on **staging** the BFF has `INGRESS_TRAFFIC_INTERNAL_ONLY` and no public invoker (TD38), so the check *records* that Asaas cannot reach the webhook path there and that staging is verified through `simulate` and recorded payloads (S13, S37); on **production** the check is that the webhook path is reachable through the edge load balancer and that Ikaro's outbound calls to Asaas leave through the configured egress (`gcloud run services describe <bff> --format='value(spec.template.metadata.annotations)'` read against § Cloud Run `vpc_egress` mode) — observed in S39 with the production smoke charge, not here. Pass = (1)–(3) observed on staging and (4) recorded for staging with the production half carried to S39.

**Files to create/modify:**
- `apps/backend/src/config/env.validation.ts` (+ its spec) (modify)
- `apps/bff/src/config/env.validation.ts` (+ its spec) (modify)
- `packages/infra-scripts/src/env-contract.ts` (+ its spec) (modify — `exemptKeys` for the two Asaas vars)
- `infra/terraform/modules/secrets/main.tf` and `infra/terraform/modules/secrets/tests/resources_unit_test.tftest.hcl` (modify — `base_secret_ids` gains `asaas-api-key`, `asaas-webhook-token`; the test fixture lists every secret)
- `infra/terraform/envs/staging/main.tf`, `infra/terraform/envs/prod/main.tf` (modify — `secret_env_vars` wiring in `cloudrun_backend` / `cloudrun_bff` and the `FEATURE_FLAG_BILLING_PROVIDER_LIVE` `env_vars` entry; prod's `bootstrap_mode` ignores `secret_env_vars` until go-live)
- `infra/terraform/foundation/modules/runtime-identities/main.tf` and `infra/terraform/foundation/modules/runtime-identities/tests/runtime_identities_unit_test.tftest.hcl` (modify, PR2 — `secret_accessors_base` backend and BFF lists, test fixture)
- `infra/terraform/foundation/envs/staging/main.tf`, `infra/terraform/foundation/envs/prod/main.tf` (modify, PR2 — the hard-coded `secret_ids` maps)
- `infra/terraform/SECRETS.md` (modify — document the new entries and the rotation order)
- `.env.example` of the backend and the BFF (modify — the new vars; local values: flag `true`, empty secrets)
- `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_INFRA.md` § Billing configuration (planned) (modify only if the implemented variable names differ from the doc; the docs owner adds the flag)

**Acceptance criteria — product:**
- [ ] No tenant-visible change.
- [ ] In `production` with `FEATURE_FLAG_BILLING_PROVIDER_LIVE=true` the backend and BFF refuse to start without the Asaas secrets; with the flag `false` (the pre-KYC production default) they start without them and the registry refuses live profile keys.

**Acceptance criteria — technical:**
- Unit:
  - [ ] Each env schema entry (`ASAAS_API_KEY`, `ASAAS_WEBHOOK_TOKEN`, `FEATURE_FLAG_BILLING_PROVIDER_LIVE`): present / missing / malformed; required exactly when the flag is `true`; the flag defaults `false` in `production` and `true` elsewhere
  - [ ] The refusal of a fake/sandbox key in `production` and of a live key outside it stays the registry's test in M25-S14 (not duplicated here)
- Integration: none — configuration only
- Tenant isolation: none — no tenant data
- E2E: none
- [ ] Checkov / Tfsec clean; `terraform test` of `modules/secrets` and `runtime-identities` green with the new fixtures
- [ ] `env-contract` check passes (`pnpm --filter @ikaro/architecture-check check`)
- [ ] Coverage ≥80% on changed code
- [ ] `tsc --noEmit` clean, lint clean

### M26-S02 — `Charge` aggregate, `ChargeLine`, provider-event inbox, recovery-charge factory

**Agent:** `backend-ts`
**Complexity:** L
**Docs to load:** `docs/DOMAIN_MODEL.md` § Aggregate: Charge and § Context 7: Billing Context (price snapshot, immutability), `docs/DATABASE_SCHEMA.md` § `billing.charges`, `billing.charge_lines`, `billing.provider_events`, `docs/LOCK_CATALOG.md` § Billing locks (B-R1; the open-recovery unique), `docs/TESTING_STRATEGY.md` § Integration test DB isolation (entity registration in the harness), `docs/USE_CASES.md` UC-113 (main flow 1–3), UC-114 (steps 2, 4), UC-134 (main flow), `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_BACKEND.md` § Billing (planned) (provider webhooks, PENDING→READY recipe) and § Transactions, `docs/AGENT_PATTERNS.md` (aggregate / repository), `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_TESTING.md` § Shared test-builder date defaults
**Dependencies:** M25-S08 (`Subscription`, repositories, `findByTenantIdForUpdate`), M25-S04 (`FeaturePrice`, `MinorAmount`, `GetFeaturePriceAtUseCase`), M25-S12 (`IClock`), M25-S14 (`BillingProviderPort` types)
**Pattern:** Aggregate root with an immutable child snapshot; Repository + Adapter; a **domain factory** (`RecoveryChargeFactory`) shared by the sweep, reactivate and retry.

**Description:**
Give the money its model. **`Charge`** (root, `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/domain/charge.aggregate.ts`) carries kind (`PERIOD` | `RECOVERY`), period dates (null for `RECOVERY`), due date, immutable `ChargeLine[]` price snapshot, `subtotalMinor` / `amountMinor` (equal for BR; the field exists so M28 can fill tax), status, method, `paidAt`, provider ids / payment URL / expiry / `receiptUrl`, `paymentReturnSeenAt`, `refundedAmountMinor`, `providerCreationStatus`. Behaviour: `markPaid(method, paidAt, receiptUrl | null, now)` (idempotent — a second paid fact is a no-op; `receiptUrl` comes from the provider re-read, `NormalizedChargeStatus.receiptUrl` of M25-S14, and feeds `ChargePaid`), `markOverdue`, `cancel`, `markRefunded` (full or partial), `markChargeback`, `markCreationReady(providerChargeId, paymentUrl, expiresAt)`, `markCreationFailed` (increments `provider_creation_attempts`, which S05's "after N failures" alert reads; the column exists in the M25-S03 schema; it leaves `provider_creation_status` at `PENDING`, so `provider_creation_status = FAILED` is unreachable in M26 and is covered by a test as such), `recordPaymentReturn(now)`, `markProviderCancelPending()` / `clearProviderCancelPending()` (the `billing.charges.provider_cancel_pending` flag that records "cancelled locally, the provider side still to do"; S06/S19/S20 set it in the local transaction, S07 retries and clears it), `claimForProviderCreation(now)` (the creation lease, below); `FAILED` is a documented status with **no M26 transition into it** (reserved for M29's automatic collection; `docs/DOMAIN_MODEL.md`'s transition table is aligned to say so by the docs owner) and is covered by the transition table as unreachable; illegal transitions throw a typed domain error (a `PAID` charge can only become `REFUNDED` / `CHARGEBACK`; a `CANCELED` one cannot be paid by a local event — only a provider-confirmed fact, applied by S04, may revive it. **"Legal" is defined once, here:** a paid fact is legal for a `PENDING`, `OVERDUE` or `CANCELED` charge when it comes from a provider fact verified by the re-read (S04) — for a `CANCELED` charge this is exactly the case "the provider could not cancel it because it was already paid" (UC-114 step 4) and also a payer using a cancelled link before the cancel reached the provider; a paid fact for a `REFUNDED`/`CHARGEBACK` charge is illegal and only logged. The `CANCELED → PAID` edge is not in `docs/DOMAIN_MODEL.md`'s table yet; the docs owner adds it). **`ChargeLine`** is a plain immutable value (`featureKey`, `tier`, `unitPriceMinor`). **`RecoveryChargeFactory`** builds the one-month `RECOVERY` charge for a subscription: the amount fixed at issue from the price versions in force **that day**, lines = the subscription's current items, due date = today + `RECOVERY_CHARGE_DUE_DAYS` (7, `docs/DOMAIN_MODEL.md` § Context 7: Billing Context (constants) — a charge due today would be `OVERDUE` on the next sweep), no period dates. **The factory is domain code and takes already-resolved inputs** — the items, a price map (`featureKey`/`tier` → `unitPriceMinor`) and "today" as a date in the **subscription's timezone** (`subscriptions.timezone`) — so it never calls an application use case; the calling use case (S06, S20, S05's retry) resolves the prices through `GetFeaturePriceAtUseCase` (M25-S04) for that day. It is the single place the recovery rule lives (UC-115 step 3, UC-134, UC-113 A2). **`ProviderEvent`** is a thin inbox record exactly as `docs/DATABASE_SCHEMA.md` § `billing.provider_events` defines it — PK `(provider, event_id)`, `tenant_id` NOT NULL (the resolved tenant), `event_type`, `received_at`, `processed_at`; **no payload column** (the payload is not stored) — with a repository whose `insertIfAbsent` is `ON CONFLICT (provider, event_id) DO NOTHING` returning whether it inserted (this is how a replayed webhook becomes a no-op without an exception). **Repositories:** `ChargeRepository` (`save`, `findById`, `findByProviderChargeId`, `findOpenByTenant`, `findOpenRecoveryBySubscription`, `listByTenantPage` ordered `(tenant_id, due_date DESC)`, selection queries for the jobs: charges with `provider_creation_status = PENDING`, charges due soon / overdue, open charges for reconcile, charges with `provider_cancel_pending`, `claimForProviderCreation`: a short-transaction `UPDATE … WHERE provider_creation_status = 'PENDING' AND provider_charge_id IS NULL AND (provider_creation_last_attempt_at IS NULL OR provider_creation_last_attempt_at < now() - 10 minutes) … FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED RETURNING`, which sets `provider_creation_last_attempt_at = now()` and increments `provider_creation_attempts` and returns whether this caller owns the creation — the lease every creator takes before any `createCharge`, see M26-D3), all `tenant_id`-scoped except the explicitly cross-tenant job selectors, which take no tenant and are named `…ForJob`. The unique constraints created in M25-S03 are the backstops (`UNIQUE(tenant_id, subscription_id, period_start)` ignoring cancelled charges; the open-recovery unique `UQ_billing_charges_open_recovery` for open recovery charges); the repository maps a unique violation to a typed `ChargeAlreadyExists` / `RecoveryChargeAlreadyOpen` result the callers treat as "return the existing one". The three domain errors are internal and never reach a client as a distinct code: the mapper maps `IllegalChargeTransition` to `409` with the existing generic conflict code and the two "already" results are consumed by the use cases (no new `BillingErrorCode`, so no `@ikaro/types` or locale change in this story).

**Backend use case steps:** none — this story is the model, repositories and factory; use cases arrive with S04–S09.
**Backend HTTP surface:** none.
**New migration / i18n keys / env vars / feature flags:** none (the tables exist from M25-S03; entity mappings only).

**Files to create/modify:**
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/domain/charge.aggregate.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`) (new)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/domain/charge-line.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`) (new)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/domain/recovery-charge.factory.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`) (new)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/domain/provider-event.ts` (new)
- `apps/backend/src/test/repositories/billing/in-memory-charge.repository.ts`, `in-memory-provider-event.repository.ts` (new — `InMemoryXxx` doubles for the two ports; `no-jest-fn-for-repository-or-port`; follow the sibling naming in that directory)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/domain/errors/illegal-charge-transition.error.ts`, `charge-already-exists.error.ts`, `recovery-charge-already-open.error.ts` (new — one file per invariant error; M25-S08's error files are different names)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/http/billing-error.mapper.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`) (modify — M25-S06 created it; map the three errors as stated above, `error-mapper-coverage`)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/application/ports/charge-repository.port.ts`, `provider-event-repository.port.ts` (new)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/entities/charge.entity.ts`, `charge-line.entity.ts`, `provider-event.entity.ts` (new)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/repositories/typeorm-charge.repository.ts`, `typeorm-provider-event.repository.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`, `.integration.spec.ts`) (new)
- `apps/backend/src/test/utils/billing-integration-app.ts` (modify — M25-S04 created it; register the three new entities), `apps/backend/src/test/integration-global-setup.ts` and `apps/backend/src/test/test-datasource.ts` (modify — entity registration; the harness must stay in sync with entities/migrations)
- `apps/backend/src/test/builders/billing/index.ts` (modify — export the new builders), `apps/backend/src/test/builders/billing/charge.builder.ts`, `charge-entity.builder.ts`, `charge-line.builder.ts`, `charge-line-entity.builder.ts`, `provider-event.builder.ts`, `provider-event-entity.builder.ts` (new — default `uuidv7()` ids; date defaults relative to `Date.now()`)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/billing.module.ts` (modify)
- `packages/architecture-check/architecture-policy.json` (modify — add the three entities to the `testDataHarnessRegistrations` `entities` array)
- `docs/LOCK_CATALOG.md` (modify — name the open-recovery-unique concurrency test next to its row)
- `docs/DOMAIN_MODEL.md` § Aggregate: Charge (docs owner — transition table: `CANCELED → PAID` by a provider-confirmed fact, `FAILED` reserved for M29)

**Acceptance criteria — product:**
- [ ] No tenant-visible change; the model matches `docs/DOMAIN_MODEL.md` § Aggregate: Charge field for field.

**Acceptance criteria — technical:**
- Unit:
  - [ ] Every legal and illegal status transition of `Charge` (table-driven); `markPaid` idempotent
  - [ ] Immutability: lines and amounts cannot change after construction; only status / payment fields are written
  - [ ] `RecoveryChargeFactory`: builds from the price map it is given, lines = current items, no period dates, due date from the subscription-timezone date it is given (the "version in force today, a version effective tomorrow is ignored" case is asserted on the price-resolving call in the use-case tests of S06/S20, not here)
  - [ ] The provider-event repository's `insertIfAbsent` returns `false` on a duplicate and stores the tenant id and event type, never a payload
  - [ ] `markPaid` stores `receiptUrl` (and `null` when the provider gave none); `markProviderCancelPending` / `clearProviderCancelPending` toggle the flag and are illegal on a `PAID` charge; a paid fact on a `CANCELED` charge is accepted only when marked provider-confirmed, and on a `REFUNDED`/`CHARGEBACK` one is rejected
  - [ ] Recovery charge due date = today + 7 (boundary test)
- Integration:
  - [ ] `UNIQUE(tenant_id, subscription_id, period_start)` rejects a second period charge and ignores a cancelled one
  - [ ] **Concurrency (`UQ_billing_charges_open_recovery`):** two simultaneous `RecoveryChargeFactory` saves for one subscription produce exactly one open recovery charge; the loser reads the existing one
  - [ ] `provider_events` duplicate insert is a no-op under concurrency
  - [ ] **Concurrency (creation lease):** two simultaneous `claimForProviderCreation` calls on one `PENDING` charge give exactly one owner; a claim within 10 minutes of the last attempt is refused, a later one succeeds
  - [ ] Pagination order and the job selectors use their standalone indexes (`EXPLAIN` asserted)
- Tenant isolation: [ ] a tenant-scoped finder never returns another tenant's charge; a composite FK `(tenant_id, id)` rejects a cross-tenant line
- E2E: none — covered by unit / integration
- [ ] Coverage ≥80% on changed code
- [ ] `tsc --noEmit` clean, lint clean

### M26-S03 — Billing domain events, audit-log lines and topics

**Agent:** `backend-ts + devops`
**Complexity:** M
**Docs to load:** `docs/DOMAIN_EVENTS.md` § Billing Context Events (every payload), `docs/ANTI_PATTERNS.md` § A domain event is drained, `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_BACKEND.md` § Event Handlers (Pub/Sub consumers), `docs/BOUNDED_CONTEXTS.md` § 7. Billing Context (published events), `infra/terraform/README.md` § New-resource PR-sequencing playbook (row: "A new Pub/Sub topic …"), `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_SHARED.md` § Tenant access enforcement (item on event subscriptions — audit handlers always run)
**Dependencies:** M25-S15b (created `SubscriptionSuspended` / `SubscriptionReactivated`, the outbox publishing pattern and `BillingAuditLogHandler`), M26-S02 (charge types the payloads refer to), M25-S21b (every `subscribe()` declares `skipWhenSuspended`)
**Pattern:** Domain events through the outbox; one audit-log subscription line per event (the `domain-event-audit-coverage` detector); topics provisioned from `pubsub-catalog.json`.

**Description:**
Define the Billing events M26's use cases publish, **before** any use case publishes them, so each use-case story is not also a topic story and the devops work is done once. New domain events (envelope per CLAUDE.md §4: `eventId` uuid-v7, `tenantId`, `occurredAt`, `correlationId`, `eventName`, `eventVersion`, `data` — exact `data` per `docs/DOMAIN_EVENTS.md`): `ChargeIssued` (charge id, kind, amount, due date, **`paymentUrl`** as `docs/DOMAIN_EVENTS.md` carries it — the email needs the link and Notification reads only event data; every email-triggering event also carries the nullable `recipientEmail`, see below), `ChargePaid` (charge id, amount, method, paidAt and the **provider receipt URL** `receiptUrl` (nullable) — `docs/DOMAIN_EVENTS.md` gains it, because the receipt email links to it; S02's `markPaid` stores it and S04 fills it from the provider re-read), `SubscriptionPastDue`, `SubscriptionCancelled` (`immediate`, `cancelAt`), `PlanChanged`, `SubscriptionPriceChangeAnnounced` (the consolidated per-tenant payload; published only by S38 but defined here so the topic exists once). `SubscriptionSuspended` / `SubscriptionReactivated` already exist (M25-S15b); this story only adds `recipientEmail` to them (their `data` is otherwise unchanged). **`recipientEmail` — the single documented exception to "no email in an event payload":** the email-triggering events (`ChargeIssued`, `ChargePaid`, `SubscriptionPastDue`, `SubscriptionSuspended`, `SubscriptionReactivated`, `SubscriptionCancelled`, `PlanChanged`, `SubscriptionPriceChangeAnnounced`) and the `BillingReminderDue` command carry `recipientEmail: string | null`, resolved by **Billing** at publish time from `BillingAccount.billingEmail` (null when unset) through one shared helper the publishing use cases call. Notification never reads Billing: it uses the field when non-null and otherwise falls back to the tenant's MANAGER emails (`INotificationStaffPort.getManagerEmails`, S12). It is the only email in any Billing payload (no tax id, no address, no other email field), it is excluded from the command's dedupe key, and it adds no cross-context edge. **Exempt subscriptions emit no email-triggering event or command at all, and an event raised on an exempt path (e.g. the operator `exempt` set reactivating a suspended tenant) carries `recipientEmail = null` — the shared helper returns `null` for an `exempt` subscription and the use cases skip publishing the email-only events for it.** Billing also re-checks "charge still open" and "not exempt" immediately before writing the outbox row of any email-triggering event or command (Notification does no re-check and has no read port into Billing; a small stale window is accepted and the email copy is tolerant, S12). Each event gets its class in `domain/events/`, a publisher call-site contract (the use cases call `outbox.add(...)` inside their transaction), and a line in `BillingAuditLogHandler` (`subscribe(<Event>.name, …, LogDomainEventUseCase.CONSUMER_NAME, { skipWhenSuspended: false })` — the call site is what gives the event a topic, and the fourth argument is mandatory since M25-S21b: audit-log and Billing's own handlers always run for a suspended tenant). **`BillingReminderDue` is a Command, not a domain event** — the class-versus-command question left open in the promotion is settled here: it is emitted by the reminders job (S09) and its idempotency key is a *business key* `(subscription, kind, daysOffset, chargeId)`, which is exactly what `shared.outbox.dedup_key` carries for a `Command` (`docs/DATABASE_SCHEMA.md` § `shared.outbox`: "the event's `eventId` for a `DomainEvent`, or the caller-supplied business key for a `Command`"; precedent `BookingReminderDue`, published through the outbox like the other cron-emitted messages). It therefore gets `domain/commands/billing-reminder-due.command.ts` and **no** audit-log line (the detector covers `DomainEvent`s). Its **topic is created by its consumer's `subscribe()` call site in S12** (topics are generated from `subscribe()` / `registerTrigger()` call sites, so nothing in S03 can give the command a topic). `docs/DOMAIN_EVENTS.md` already marks the entry as a Command and already carries `recipientEmail?` on every email-triggering event; the remaining doc work (`ChargePaid.receiptUrl`, the five reminder kinds, the "Billing re-checks at publish time" wording) is done by the docs owner, and this story only corrects any implemented payload that differs.

**Devops half (six new event topics).** Each new domain event needs a topic and the audit-log subscription; the reminder command's topic arrives with its consumer in S12. **PR sequence:** 1 PR + 1 Foundation apply per the playbook row "A new Pub/Sub topic (domain event …) + its app code" — PR1 (`infra-app-mix-ok`): the six event classes, the one command class (no topic yet), `BillingAuditLogHandler` lines for the six events, `pubsub-catalog.json` regenerated (`pnpm --filter @ikaro/infra-scripts run pubsub-catalog`); no `envs/*` edit (`modules/pubsub` reads the catalog directly, M25-S15b precedent); then the manual Foundation apply (`foundation-deploy.yml`, `apply=true`, from `main`).
**IAM / permissions:** publisher = the backend service account (as every domain-event topic); subscriber = the audit-log push subscription's invoker. No new principals.
**Live-verification check:** after the Foundation apply, `gcloud pubsub topics get-iam-policy projects/<staging>/topics/ikaro-ChargeIssued` (and the other five topics) list the publisher and subscriber bindings in **both** projects — an empty `{"etag": …}` means it was never applied (M23-S04 failure mode); then publish one event through the S13 `simulate`/seed path on staging once available, or a one-off backend REPL call, and confirm the audit-log consumer logs it. Pass = all six policies populated and one consumer log observed.

**Backend use case steps:** none (no use case yet publishes; S04–S09 and S19–S20 do).
**Backend HTTP surface:** none.
**New migration / i18n keys / env vars / feature flags:** none.

**Files to create/modify:**
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/domain/events/charge-issued.event.ts`, `charge-paid.event.ts`, `subscription-past-due.event.ts`, `subscription-cancelled.event.ts`, `plan-changed.event.ts`, `subscription-price-change-announced.event.ts` (+ `.spec.ts` each) (new)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/domain/commands/billing-reminder-due.command.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`) (new)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/domain/events/subscription-suspended.event.ts`, `subscription-reactivated.event.ts` (+ specs) (modify — M25-S15b created them; add `recipientEmail`)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/events/billing-audit-log.handler.ts` (+ spec) (modify — M25-S15b created it)
- `infra/terraform/pubsub-catalog.json` (modify — regenerated)
- `apps/backend/src/test/builders/billing/charge-issued-event.builder.ts`, `charge-paid-event.builder.ts`, `subscription-past-due-event.builder.ts`, `subscription-cancelled-event.builder.ts`, `plan-changed-event.builder.ts`, `subscription-price-change-announced-event.builder.ts`, `billing-reminder-due-command.builder.ts`, and `subscription-suspended-event.builder.ts` / `subscription-reactivated-event.builder.ts` if M25-S15b did not create them (new; `test-builder-coverage` requires an `<Event>EventBuilder` / `<Command>CommandBuilder` once a class is constructed inline in two spec files — S04–S12 will), plus `apps/backend/src/test/builders/billing/index.ts` (modify)
- `docs/DOMAIN_EVENTS.md`, `docs/BOUNDED_CONTEXTS.md` (`ChargePaid` carrying `receiptUrl` is already in `DOMAIN_EVENTS` — nothing to add; modify only where an implemented payload or the re-check wording differs from the doc)

**Acceptance criteria — product:**
- [ ] No tenant-visible change.

**Acceptance criteria — technical:**
- Unit:
  - [ ] Each event carries the full envelope and the documented `data`; no personal data in any payload except the single documented nullable `recipientEmail` (no tax id, no address, no other email field); the command's dedupe key is built from `(subscription, kind, daysOffset, chargeId)`
  - [ ] `BillingAuditLogHandler` subscribes every new domain event with an explicit `skipWhenSuspended: false` (the `domain-event-audit-coverage` and `event-handler-suspension-declaration` detectors pass)
  - [ ] The `recipientEmail` helper returns the billing email when set, `null` when unset, and `null` for an `exempt` subscription
- Integration:
  - [ ] An event added to the outbox inside a transaction is published and reaches the audit-log consumer through the in-memory bus; two outbox inserts of the command with the same dedupe key leave one row
- Tenant isolation: [ ] every event carries its own `tenantId`; a consumer sees only that tenant's data
- E2E: none — live check above
- [ ] Checkov / Tfsec clean
- [ ] Coverage ≥80% on changed code
- [ ] `tsc --noEmit` clean, lint clean
