### M26-S18 — BOOKING removal guard and the `bookableUntil` window cap (UC-136 A11)

**Agent:** `backend-ts + bff-ts`
**Complexity:** L
**Docs to load:** `docs/USE_CASES.md` UC-136 (A11), `docs/BUSINESS_LOGIC_REFERENCE.md` § Planned: the billing `bookableUntil` cap and § Booking — Booking Window (M23-S33), `docs/DOMAIN_MODEL.md` § Context 7 (removal guard), `docs/BOUNDED_CONTEXTS.md` § 7. Billing Context ("Billing → Booking"), § Rule 2 — Communication via Events or BFF Only, `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_BACKEND.md` § Layering inside `application/`, `docs/ANTI_PATTERNS.md` (new cross-context Port+Adapter when one exists), `plan/M24-MULTIVERTICAL-CLASSES-SESSIONS.md` § Billing interaction (planned)
**Dependencies:** M25-S08 (`Subscription`), M25-S13 (the access answer the cap rides on — this story edits its files, listed below). The cap is *written* by S19 (`scheduleItems` produces the pending-removal item this story derives `bookableUntil` from); S19 depends on this story, not the reverse.
**Pattern:** Port + Adapter across contexts (Billing → Booking) behind **one** use case; a pure `bookableUntil` input threaded through the existing window resolver; Strategy-free — no new abstraction beyond the input.

**Description:**
Two linked changes, both in the Booking context, so removing the BOOKING feature can never strand a commitment. **(1) The guard.** Removing `BOOKING` (which includes recurring reservations and availability alerts) is rejected while any `PENDING` / `INFO_REQUESTED` / `APPROVED` booking, `PENDING_APPROVAL` / `ACTIVE` recurring schedule or `ACTIVE` alert extends on or after the removal's **effective date** → `BILLING_FEATURE_IN_USE`, and the response names what blocks it. A new Booking use case `GetBookingFeatureRemovalImpactUseCase(tenantId, effectiveDate)` runs **three queries** — bookings ending on or after the date; recurring schedules with `endsOn >= date`; tenant-wide active alerts with `expiresAt >= date` — counting the occurrence bookings of an active schedule **once**, and returns counts plus a bounded list of blockers. **The `BILLING_FEATURE_IN_USE` problem body (defined here, thrown and translated by S19):** `params: { effectiveFrom: 'YYYY-MM-DD', bookings: number, recurringSchedules: number, alerts: number, blockers: [{ category: 'BOOKING' | 'RECURRING_SCHEDULE' | 'ALERT', id, date }] }` (at most 10 blockers; ids and dates only, no customer data), so the web (S30) can name the categories and counts and link to each. Billing reaches it through **one** port `BillingBookingPort` with an adapter in `contexts/billing/infrastructure/cross-context/billing-booking.adapter.ts` registered as an exact-path `permittedEdges` entry (grep `infrastructure/cross-context/` first: this is the first Billing → Booking edge, so no existing adapter is reusable). It is read-only and never a SQL join. **When M24 lands**, class-session bookings, enrollments and waitlist entries must be counted by the same use case — the M24 story that introduces each record extends this use case's queries in the same story (stated as an AC with a recount note, and already written into `plan/M24-…`). **(2) The cap.** Once a removal is saved, the bookable window is capped at its effective date: a new optional `bookableUntil` input is honoured by **every window consumer** (enumerated in Files from a grep of the real code) — request / authenticated / staff / reschedule booking, the recurring-schedule window helpers, the availability-alert `selectableDays` / `bookingWindow` context, the booking-by-id reschedule options, the availability summary and the manifest `booking` block — so no new commitment is accepted beyond the effective date once a removal is pending. **How the web sees the cap (decided):** the backend caps the effective `maxBookingAdvanceDays` it already returns — `effective = min(configured, whole days from today to bookableUntil in the tenant timezone)` — in the manifest `booking` block and in the reschedule-options window, so the date pickers (`apps/web/app/[slug]/booking/page.tsx` reads `manifest.booking.maxBookingAdvanceDays`; `ReschedulePicker.tsx` reads `window.maxAdvanceDays`) need **no web change** and never offer a day the server rejects. The manifest `booking` block is built by Platform's `GetHotsiteManifestUseCase` (`mapBookingSettings`), not by the BFF; Platform reads the cap through the shared `TenantAccessPort` (no new cross-context edge), and S19 revalidates the manifest after a plan save that sets or clears a cap. **Closing the check-then-act gap explicitly (decided here).** Counting under the subscription row lock is not enough: Booking's write paths do not take B-R1 (`docs/LOCK_CATALOG.md` § Billing locks: Billing never opens a Booking transaction), so a booking committed after the count but before the plan-save commit would be invisible to both the guard and the not-yet-committed cap. The order is therefore: **(a)** `SavePlanUseCase` (S19) commits the **scheduled removal and its cap first** (the scheduled item with its effective date is the pending-removal state; `bookableUntil` derives from it), invalidating the access cache after commit; **(b)** from that moment Booking's new-commitment save paths (request / authenticated / staff booking, reschedule, recurring-schedule request, alert create / update) **re-read `bookableUntil` uncached** (a cache-bypassing read of just that value — a new `TenantAccessPort` method) immediately before saving and reject beyond it, so the cached answer's TTL is irrelevant; **(c)** after (a) commits, `SavePlanUseCase` calls the removal-impact guard (this story's use case, through `BillingBookingPort`) and, **if a blocker appeared**, reverts the scheduled removal (a compensating transaction under B-R1) and returns `BILLING_FEATURE_IN_USE` naming it; the guard is also run before (a) so the common rejection never writes anything. A booking racing the save is therefore either rejected by (b) or counted by (c) — never stranded. The cap is not expressible as "days", hence a date input to the window resolver. **How Booking learns the cap — decided here: `bookableUntil` rides on the shared `TenantAccessPort` answer** (the access answer is already cached and read by every consumer; the story updates `get-tenant-access.use-case.ts`, the caching adapter, the BFF access mapper and DTO, and `docs/DOMAIN_MODEL.md` § Entry point: `TenantAccessPort` in the same change). The alternative — a Booking-owned projection updated from `PlanChanged` — is recorded and rejected for adding an event consumer, a table and a second source of truth; story discovery may revisit it only if the cached answer proves too coarse. **Non-regression contract (M26 backward-compatibility owner):** with no cap set — every tenant today — every window consumer returns exactly what it returned before.

**Backend use case steps:**
1. **`GetBookingFeatureRemovalImpactUseCase`** (Booking), 2. the cap input threaded through `resolveBookingWindow` and its consumers.
**Backend HTTP surface:** none new — the guard is called in-process by S19; the cap affects existing routes' results only. (If the `bookableUntil` source needs an internal read endpoint, story discovery adds `GET /internal/tenants/{tenantId}/booking-cap`; not assumed.)
**New migration / i18n keys / env vars / feature flags:** none.

**Files to create/modify:**
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/booking/application/use-cases/get-booking-feature-removal-impact.use-case.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`, `.integration.spec.ts`) (new)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/booking/application/ports/booking-repository.port.ts`, `recurring-booking-schedule-repository.port.ts`, `availability-alert-repository.port.ts` (modify — the three queries) and their TypeORM implementations `apps/backend/src/contexts/booking/infrastructure/repositories/typeorm-booking.repository.ts`, `typeorm-recurring-booking-schedule.repository.ts`, `typeorm-availability-alert.repository.ts` (+ specs) (modify)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/application/ports/billing-booking.port.ts` (new), `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/cross-context/billing-booking.adapter.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`, `.integration.spec.ts`) (new)
- Booking window resolver and its consumers (modify — enumerated by `grep` of `tenantBookingWindow` / `maxBookingAdvanceDays` in `apps/backend/src/contexts/booking`): under `application/use-cases/`: `booking-window.helpers.ts`, `availability-window-resolution.helpers.ts`, `resource-scoped-availability.helpers.ts`, `recurring-booking-schedule-window.helpers.ts`, `availability-alert-window.helpers.ts`, `booking-reschedule-options.helpers.ts`, `request-booking.use-case.ts`, `request-authenticated-booking.use-case.ts`, `create-booking-by-staff.use-case.ts`, `reschedule-booking-as-customer.use-case.ts`, `request-recurring-booking-schedule.use-case.ts` and `request-recurring-booking-schedule.types.ts`, `get-booking-by-id.use-case.ts` (reschedule options), `get-service-by-id.use-case.ts`, `get-services.use-case.ts`, `service-result.mapper.ts`, `create-availability-alert.use-case.ts` / `update-availability-alert.use-case.ts` (via `availability-alert-input.helpers.ts`), `get-availability-summary.use-case.ts`; `application/services/availability-alert-matching.service.ts`; `application/ports/booking-platform.port.ts` and `infrastructure/cross-context/booking-platform.adapter.ts`; **the three controllers that build `tenantBookingWindow` from `ctx.settings`** and must pass the cap: `infrastructure/controllers/booking.controller.ts`, `recurring-booking-schedule.controller.ts`, `booking-completion.controller.ts`, plus `schedule-availability-summary.controller.ts`; and in Platform `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/application/use-cases/get-hotsite-manifest.use-case.ts` (the manifest `booking` block, `mapBookingSettings`) (+ specs). The BFF has no `bookingWindow` symbol and needs no change for the cap.
- `apps/backend/src/shared/ports/tenant-access.port.ts` (modify — the access answer gains `bookableUntil: string | null` and the port gains the uncached `getBookableUntil(tenantId)` read) and `apps/backend/src/test/infrastructure/in-memory-tenant-access.port.ts` (modify — both), `apps/backend/src/test/infrastructure/in-memory-billing-booking.port.ts` (new — the `InMemory` double of `BillingBookingPort` for Billing's unit specs), and the in-memory Booking repositories the three new queries extend: `apps/backend/src/test/repositories/booking/in-memory-booking.repository.ts`, `in-memory-recurring-booking-schedule.repository.ts` and `in-memory-availability-alert.repository.ts`
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/application/use-cases/get-tenant-access.use-case.ts`, `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/adapters/caching-tenant-access.adapter.ts` (+ specs) (modify — carry `bookableUntil` on the cached answer; implement the uncached `getBookableUntil` that reads the subscription's pending-removal item directly; M25-S13 created these files)
- `apps/bff/src/features/billing/billing.mapper.ts` (+ spec) (modify — the role-reduced `GET /v1/billing/access` view must **omit** `bookableUntil` (internal; the web learns the cap only through the clamped window), so the mapper drops it and a spec asserts it); `billing.types.ts` and `packages/types/src/billing.dto.ts` only if the backend answer type is mirrored there
- `packages/architecture-check/architecture-policy.json` (modify — one exact-path `permittedEdges` entry)
- `docs/DOMAIN_MODEL.md`, `docs/BUSINESS_LOGIC_REFERENCE.md`, `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` (modify — `docs/DOMAIN_MODEL.md` § Entry point: `TenantAccessPort` gains `bookableUntil` and the uncached read; `docs/BUSINESS_LOGIC_REFERENCE.md` final source, the web-side clamping and the three-step order; `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` notes that `GET /v1/billing/access` does not expose it; remove "Planned" once built)

**Acceptance criteria — product:**
- [ ] A tenant cannot remove BOOKING while it has live bookings, recurring schedules or alerts reaching the removal date, and is told exactly what blocks it.
- [ ] After a removal is saved, no new booking, recurring schedule or alert can be created beyond the effective date, in any channel (guest, customer, staff, reschedule, recurring, alert).
- [ ] A tenant with no scheduled removal sees no change in any booking window.
- [ ] Decision recorded: the cap rides on the shared access answer (cached) plus one uncached read at save time; the alternative (a Booking-owned projection fed by `PlanChanged`) was rejected for adding a consumer, a table and a second source of truth.

**Acceptance criteria — technical:**
- Unit:
  - [ ] The three queries' boundary (a booking ending exactly on the effective date blocks; one ending the day before does not); occurrence bookings of an active schedule counted once
  - [ ] Window resolver: `bookableUntil` clamps each consumer; `null` equals today's result (table-driven across all consumers)
- Integration:
  - [ ] **Non-regression:** the existing window / availability / recurring / alert integration suites pass unchanged, plus a test that a `null` cap equals today's result
  - [ ] **Race after the cap commits:** a booking, recurring schedule or alert created after the scheduled removal committed but before the cached access answer expires is rejected beyond the effective date (Booking's save re-reads `bookableUntil` uncached)
  - [ ] **Race before the cap commits (pre-commit):** a booking beyond the effective date committed after the guard's first count but before the plan-save commit is caught by the post-commit re-count, the scheduled removal is reverted and `BILLING_FEATURE_IN_USE` is returned (test in S19's integration suite using this story's use case)
  - [ ] The manifest `booking.maxBookingAdvanceDays` and the reschedule-options `window.maxAdvanceDays` are clamped to the whole days until `bookableUntil` in the tenant timezone, and unchanged when it is `null`
  - [ ] End to end: a tenant with an `APPROVED` booking past the date → `BILLING_FEATURE_IN_USE` with the blocker named; after the booking is cancelled the guard passes
  - [ ] The adapter is the only Billing → Booking import (`architecture-check` cross-context detector passes with the one new exact-path entry)
- Tenant isolation: [ ] counts are strictly per `tenant_id`; another tenant's bookings never block this tenant
- E2E: none — covered by integration
- [ ] Coverage ≥80% on changed code
- [ ] `tsc --noEmit` clean, lint clean

### M26-S19 — Plan save and trial confirm (UC-136, UC-130)

**Agent:** `backend-ts + bff-ts`
**Complexity:** L
**Docs to load:** `docs/USE_CASES.md` UC-136 (main flow 3, A1–A13), UC-130 (first charge), UC-113 (A10), `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` § Billing — Manager API (`PUT /plan`, `POST /plan/confirm`), `docs/DOMAIN_MODEL.md` § Aggregate: Subscription (scheduling, `plan_confirmed_at`), `docs/LOCK_CATALOG.md` § Billing locks (B-R1 order), `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_BACKEND.md` § Billing (planned) and § Transactions
**Dependencies:** M26-S05 (`IssueChargeForSubscriptionUseCase` and the provider-creation claim), M26-S06 (`CancelOpenChargesUseCase`, the recovery/provider-cancel steps and `provider_cancel_pending`), M26-S16 (address completeness), M26-S17 (selection validation, preview and the `nextPeriodPaid` rule), M26-S18 (removal guard port, the cap and the uncached read), M26-S03 (`PlanChanged` event), M26-S04 (`ApplyChargeFactUseCase` for the "just paid" path)
**Pattern:** Command use case with B-R1 (subscription row lock) → charge rows; cancel-and-re-issue after commit with provider I/O outside the transaction (M26-D3).

**Description:**
Persist the manager's plan choice. **`PUT /v1/billing/plan`** `{ items: [{ featureKey, tier }] }`: under `findByTenantIdForUpdate` it validates the proposed **final dependency graph** (A1), non-empty (A5), a list price exists for the country/tier (A6), the full billing address is saved (A10, `BILLING_ADDRESS_REQUIRED`), the subscription state allows it (`PAST_DUE`/`SUSPENDED` and a scheduled cancel are rejected as read-only — A2/A4; trial: all features stay active, the selection only fixes the first paid period — A3) and, when `BOOKING` is removed, the removal guard through the port (S18, A11). It then **schedules** the item / tier changes at the next paid service-period start (current entitlements are unchanged) and publishes `PlanChanged`. If the next invoice is already issued and **unpaid**, it cancels and re-issues it with the provider call **after commit**. **In one transaction (under B-R1):** the old charge goes `CANCELED` with `provider_cancel_pending = true` (when it has a provider id), **and the replacement `PERIOD` charge is inserted `PENDING` with the same `period_start`, the new lines / amount (price versions in force on that period's start) and `provider_creation_status = PENDING`, without advancing `next_due_date`** (it was already advanced when the original was issued; `UQ_billing_charges_period` ignores cancelled rows, so the same `period_start` is legal). Nothing is ever left without a charge for the period, so a crash after commit loses nothing: S05's creation retry creates the replacement at the provider (A8, alert on repeated failure). **After commit, outside any transaction:** `CancelOpenChargesUseCase` (S06) cancels the old charge at the provider and clears `provider_cancel_pending` on success (S07 retries it otherwise), then the replacement is claimed and created at the provider through S05's creation step (M26-D3 / the S05 claim), raising `ChargeIssued` after `READY`. If the provider refuses to cancel the old charge because it was just paid (typed `ChargeAlreadyPaidError`, A12), that old charge **is the payment** — it is applied through `ApplyChargeFactUseCase` (S04; the local `CANCELED` is legally revived by a provider-confirmed paid fact), the replacement is cancelled locally, the selection is **re-dated**: the scheduled items that were set to start at that period's start are moved to start at the following period start (a second, short B-R1 transaction; `previewSelection`'s `nextPeriodPaid` rule, S17) and the response says the change applies from the following renewal. A **paid** invoice found at save time is treated the same way without any provider call: items start at the period after the paid one (A7). A cancel of the old charge racing an in-flight provider creation is handled where the READY step lives (S05 / `Charge.markCreationReady`, S02): storing ids on a `CANCELED` charge sets `provider_cancel_pending`, so the orphan provider charge is cancelled by S07's retry; this story owns the race test (below). **`POST /v1/billing/plan/confirm`** (trial only): confirms the current default selection unchanged, sets `plan_confirmed_at` and returns `200 { planConfirmedAt }`; `409 BILLING_PLAN_ALREADY_CONFIRMED` on a confirmed trial (A13); when the trial end is within the 10-day lead it triggers first-invoice issuing through the same per-subscription use case after commit. During the trial, `PUT /plan` also sets `plan_confirmed_at` (the same action, labelled "Confirmar plano"). **Other save-time rules:** `exempt` subscriptions are rejected `BILLING_PLAN_CHANGE_NOT_ALLOWED` (UC-117 A7); the access cache is invalidated after commit (`TenantAccessCacheInvalidator`) because `bookableUntil` rides on the access answer (S18), and when the save sets or clears a BOOKING cap the hotsite manifest is revalidated best-effort after commit (through the existing frontend-revalidation port; its `booking` block carries the clamped window); re-saving replaces any previously scheduled, not-yet-started items of the same features (the GIST exclusion is the backstop). **Response (`PUT /plan`, defined here, written into `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md`):** `200 { planConfirmedAt: string | null, effectiveFrom: 'YYYY-MM-DD', deferredBecauseNextInvoicePaid: boolean, newTotalMinor: number, openCharge: { id, amountMinor, status, hasPaymentLink } | null }`. **Rejection statuses (defaults, confirmed at story discovery):** `BILLING_FEATURE_DEPENDENCY_UNMET`, `…_HAS_DEPENDENTS`, `…_PLAN_EMPTY`, `…_NO_PRICE_FOR_COUNTRY` → `422`; `BILLING_FEATURE_IN_USE` → `409` (params per S18); `BILLING_PLAN_CHANGE_NOT_ALLOWED` → `409`; `BILLING_ADDRESS_REQUIRED` → `422` (code owned by S15); `BILLING_PLAN_ALREADY_CONFIRMED` → `409`.

**Backend use case steps:**
1. **`SavePlanUseCase`**, 2. **`ConfirmPlanUseCase`**; both end with the after-commit provider re-issue/issue hook. `SavePlanUseCase` runs the S18 three-step order for a BOOKING removal: guard, commit the scheduled removal, post-commit re-count with revert on a new blocker.
**Backend HTTP surface:** extends `billing.controller.ts` — `PUT /billing/plan`, `POST /billing/plan/confirm`.
**BFF endpoint spec:** extends the BFF billing controller — `PUT /v1/billing/plan` (body as preview), `POST /v1/billing/plan/confirm` (`@Roles('MANAGER')`, `@NoFeatureGate()`, `@SuspensionAccess(MANAGER_BILLING)`); the backend routes carry the same decorators.
**New migration / i18n keys / env vars / feature flags:** error codes first thrown here, each added to `BillingErrorCode` and both `errors.json` files: `BILLING_FEATURE_IN_USE`, `BILLING_PLAN_CHANGE_NOT_ALLOWED`, `BILLING_NO_PRICE_FOR_COUNTRY` (unless M25-S10 already added it — grep first), `BILLING_PLAN_ALREADY_CONFIRMED`. `BILLING_ADDRESS_REQUIRED` is S15's; `BILLING_FEATURE_DEPENDENCY_UNMET` / `…_HAS_DEPENDENTS` / `…_PLAN_EMPTY` are S17's.

**Files to create/modify:**
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/application/use-cases/save-plan.use-case.ts`, `confirm-plan.use-case.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`, `.integration.spec.ts`) (new)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/domain/subscription.aggregate.ts` (+ spec) (modify — `scheduleItems`, `confirmPlan`)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/http/billing-error.mapper.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`) (modify — plan rejection errors)
- `packages/types/src/error-codes.ts`, `packages/i18n/locales/pt-BR/errors.json`, `packages/i18n/locales/en/errors.json` (modify — the four codes above), `apps/web/shared/lib/i18n/error-codes-exhaustiveness.spec.ts` (only if needed)
- `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` (modify — `PUT /plan` and `POST /plan/confirm` response shapes and rejection statuses)
- `docs/LOCK_CATALOG.md` (modify — name the plan save × issue job race test)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/controllers/billing.controller.ts` (+ specs), `apps/backend/http/billing/billing.http` (modify)
- `apps/bff/src/features/billing/billing.controller.ts`, `billing.schemas.ts`, `billing.types.ts`, `billing.mapper.ts` (+ specs), `apps/bff/http/billing/billing.http` (modify)
- `packages/types/src/billing.dto.ts` (modify)

**Acceptance criteria — product:**
- [ ] A manager can change the next-period plan; the change starts at the next paid period, current access is unchanged, and an unpaid next invoice is replaced with the new amount.
- [ ] During the trial, "Confirmar plano" fixes the first invoice's content; confirming twice is refused; an unconfirmed plan is invoiced from the default selection three days before the end (S05), which the banner and emails say.
- [ ] Removing BOOKING is refused with the blocker named; an empty plan, an unmet dependency and a missing address are refused with their specific messages.

**Acceptance criteria — technical:**
- Unit:
  - [ ] Each of A1–A13 (one named test each), including trial semantics
- Integration:
  - [ ] **Concurrency (B-R1):** a plan save racing the issue job (S05) on the same subscription serialises — exactly one charge for the period carrying the final selection
  - [ ] Unpaid next invoice cancelled and re-issued with the new amount on the fake; the old one stays as `CANCELED` history; **the cancel and the replacement `PENDING` charge (same `period_start`, no `next_due_date` advance) commit in one transaction** — a simulated crash right after commit leaves a `PENDING` replacement that S05's retry creates (no period without a charge); provider failure keeps the schedule and the retry fixes it
  - [ ] "Refuses to cancel — already paid" (fake `ChargeAlreadyPaidError`): the old charge is applied as the payment, the replacement is cancelled, the scheduled items are re-dated to the following period start and the response says `deferredBecauseNextInvoicePaid`
  - [ ] **Race:** a plan save cancelling a charge whose provider creation is in flight leaves the orphan provider charge marked `provider_cancel_pending` and cancelled by S07's retry
  - [ ] **Pre-commit booking race (S18):** a booking beyond the effective date committed between the guard's count and the save commit → post-commit re-count reverts the scheduled removal and returns `BILLING_FEATURE_IN_USE`
  - [ ] `exempt` → `BILLING_PLAN_CHANGE_NOT_ALLOWED`; the access cache is invalidated after commit and the manifest revalidated when a cap is set or cleared
  - [ ] Backend and BFF routes carry `@NoFeatureGate()` + `@SuspensionAccess(MANAGER_BILLING)` (decorator spec)
  - [ ] Paid invoice never changed; `PlanChanged` published once per save that changes the selection (`added[]` / `removed[]` relative to the previously effective-or-scheduled selection); `POST /plan/confirm` of the untouched default publishes **no** `PlanChanged` (nothing changed); confirm idempotency (second → 409)
  - [ ] No provider I/O inside the transaction (`transactional-io`)
- Tenant isolation: [ ] a save affects only the caller's tenant's items and charges
- E2E: none — covered by integration; the tab E2E is S35
- [ ] Coverage ≥80% on changed code
- [ ] `tsc --noEmit` clean, lint clean

### M26-S20 — Cancel and reactivate (UC-133, UC-134)

**Agent:** `backend-ts + bff-ts`
**Complexity:** L
**Docs to load:** `docs/USE_CASES.md` UC-133 and UC-134 (all), UC-114 (step 4), UC-115 (A3), `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` § Billing — Manager API (`cancel`, `reactivate`), `docs/DOMAIN_MODEL.md` § Aggregate: Subscription and § Aggregate: Charge (recovery charge), `docs/LOCK_CATALOG.md` § Billing locks (B-R1; the open-recovery unique), `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_BACKEND.md` § Billing (planned)
**Dependencies:** M25-S15b (the operator touchpoints, `SetTenantExemptUseCase`), M26-S02 (recovery factory, charges), M26-S03 (`SubscriptionCancelled`), M26-S04 (`ApplyChargeFactUseCase` for the already-paid case), M26-S05 (the provider-creation step and claim, reached through S06), M26-S06 (suspension and recovery behaviour it must agree with, **and `CancelOpenChargesUseCase` / the recovery provider-creation step it reuses**), M26-S13 (`SeedBillingStatesUseCase`, extended here with the two cancel states)
**Pattern:** Two command use cases under B-R1; the shared `RecoveryChargeFactory` (given resolved unit prices by the use case) and S06's `CancelOpenChargesUseCase`; PENDING → READY creation for the recovery charge (M26-D3).

**Description:**
**Cancel** (`POST /v1/billing/cancel`, any manager; nothing is refunded): `ACTIVE` → set `cancelAt` = **the end of the last paid period**, computed as `max(period_end)` over the subscription's `PAID` `PERIOD` charges, falling back to `current_period_end` (invoices are issued 10 days early, so the next period may already be paid and `current_period_end` alone would cut off a paid month); cancel immediately every unpaid charge for a period beginning on or after that date (locally with `provider_cancel_pending`, the provider call after commit through `CancelOpenChargesUseCase`, S06), response `200 { cancelAt }`; `PAST_DUE` → immediate: cancel any open charge and become `SUSPENDED` (`CANCELED`), `200 { status: "SUSPENDED", cancelAt: null }`; `TRIALING` with the first `PERIOD` charge already `PAID` → `cancelAt` = **the end of that first paid period** (the same `max(period_end)` rule; access continues through the trial and the paid month, nothing is refunded, and the sweep's trial → `ACTIVE` conversion still happens at trial end, the cancel then takes effect at period end — owner decision R5; a `cancelAt = trialEndsAt` would cut off the month the customer paid for); `TRIALING` with nothing paid → immediate suspension (A3, canonical). Already cancelling → `409 BILLING_ALREADY_CANCELLING` (A1; default status `409`). A `SUSPENDED` or `exempt` subscription cannot be cancelled: `BILLING_CANCEL_NOT_ALLOWED` (default `409`; new code owned here — nothing to cancel). If the provider cancel fails, the local state is saved and reconcile (S07) retries — the manager sees success only after the local save (A2). `SubscriptionCancelled` on every cancel (emailed to the billing email by S12 — and, for an immediate cancel, the `SubscriptionSuspended` (CANCELED) manager email is suppressed there so the manager gets one email); `SubscriptionSuspended` for the immediate cases; the access cache is invalidated after commit. **Reactivate** (`POST /v1/billing/reactivate`): not allowed for an `OPERATOR` suspension (only the operator lifts it; `BILLING_REACTIVATE_NOT_ALLOWED`, default `409`, new code owned here — distinct from the operator route's `BILLING_UNSUSPEND_NOT_ALLOWED`) or after the data is purged (a new signup is needed, A1; same code). Reactivate on a subscription that is neither cancelling nor `SUSPENDED` (nothing to reactivate) → `BILLING_REACTIVATE_NOT_ALLOWED` too. **Within the paid period, `clearCancel` clears `cancelAt` and restores billing for the period it had cancelled:** cancel cancelled any unpaid charge for a period beginning on or after `cancelAt`, but `next_due_date` had already been advanced past it when that charge was issued, so the issue job would never select that period again; `clearCancel` therefore re-creates the cancelled period's `PERIOD` charge in the same transaction (`PENDING`, same `period_start`, lines at the versions in force on that period's start, no `next_due_date` advance; the provider creation follows after commit through S05's claim) when that period is within the issue lead or already issued, and otherwise simply leaves `next_due_date` for the issue job. If `SUSPENDED` (`NON_PAYMENT`, `PAYMENT_REVERSED` or `CANCELED`): cancel any open `PERIOD` charge (locally and at the provider, never re-dated) and issue **one** `RECOVERY` charge for one month through S02's factory — amount fixed at issue from the versions in force that day — `PENDING` then `READY` after the provider call (outside the transaction, through the shared creation step of S05 / S06 with its claim; provider failure → `BILLING_PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE` (default `503`) with retry, A3). **A manager reactivate with an incomplete billing identity or address returns `BILLING_ADDRESS_REQUIRED` (default `422`, code owned by S15) and creates and cancels nothing** (UC-134 A6 — nobody is "away" here, the manager can fix it now; the only held recovery charge is the one the status sweep creates, S06); response `200 { cancelAt: null, openCharge: { …, kind: "RECOVERY" } | null }` — **never a URL** (the web then calls `payment-link`). Calling reactivate again while a recovery charge is open returns that charge (the unique makes it exactly one). If the provider refuses to cancel the old charge because it was just paid, **that charge is the payment** and reactivates the subscription through `ApplyChargeFactUseCase`; the recovery charge is cancelled (A4). On payment (S04) the status becomes `ACTIVE`, the period starts at the payment, `anchor_day` is the payment's tenant-local day clamped to 28, and no months are owed for the gap. **Open-charge cancellation is one shared use case, `CancelOpenChargesUseCase`, built in S06** (local `CANCELED` + `provider_cancel_pending` in the caller's transaction, provider cancel after commit, S07 retries; a provider that reports the charge already paid goes through `ApplyChargeFactUseCase`) — this story, S19 and **the operator's exempt-set hook** call it, and does not rebuild it. A cancel racing an in-flight provider creation is handled where the READY step lives (a `CANCELED` charge that receives provider ids gets `provider_cancel_pending`). M25-S15b's `SetTenantExemptUseCase` left a no-op hook for "exempt cancels any open charge locally and at the provider" (UC-137 step 1) and this story fills it in `set-tenant-exempt.use-case.ts`.

**Backend use case steps:**
1. **`CancelSubscriptionUseCase`**, 2. **`ReactivateSubscriptionUseCase`**.
**Backend HTTP surface:** extends `billing.controller.ts` — `POST /billing/cancel`, `POST /billing/reactivate`.
**BFF endpoint spec:** extends the BFF billing controller — `POST /v1/billing/cancel`, `POST /v1/billing/reactivate` (`@Roles('MANAGER')`, `@NoFeatureGate()`, `@SuspensionAccess(MANAGER_BILLING)`); no request body. The backend routes carry the same decorators.
**New migration / i18n keys / env vars / feature flags:** error codes first thrown here, each added to `BillingErrorCode` and both `errors.json` files: `BILLING_ALREADY_CANCELLING`, `BILLING_CANCEL_NOT_ALLOWED`, `BILLING_REACTIVATE_NOT_ALLOWED`. `BILLING_ADDRESS_REQUIRED` and `BILLING_PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE` are S15's. Docs: `docs/ERROR_CATALOG.md` entries for `BILLING_CANCEL_NOT_ALLOWED` and `BILLING_REACTIVATE_NOT_ALLOWED` (both `409`) are added in the audit fix; confirm status at discovery.

**Files to create/modify:**
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/application/use-cases/cancel-subscription.use-case.ts`, `reactivate-subscription.use-case.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`, `.integration.spec.ts`) (new; `CancelOpenChargesUseCase` is S06's)
- `packages/types/src/error-codes.ts`, `packages/i18n/locales/pt-BR/errors.json`, `packages/i18n/locales/en/errors.json` (modify — the three codes), `apps/web/shared/lib/i18n/error-codes-exhaustiveness.spec.ts` (only if needed)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/application/use-cases/seed-billing-states.use-case.ts` (+ specs) (modify — S13 created it; add the two states that need a cancel: `ACTIVE` with a scheduled cancel and `SUSPENDED` for `CANCELED`)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/application/use-cases/set-tenant-exempt.use-case.ts` (+ spec) (modify — M25-S15b created it and left the documented no-op hook (M25 S15b: "in M25 no charge can exist, so this is a no-op hook that M26-S20 fills in"); fill it by calling S06's `CancelOpenChargesUseCase`: setting `exempt` cancels every open charge locally and at the provider, and publishes no email-triggering event)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/http/billing-error.mapper.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`) (modify)
- `docs/LOCK_CATALOG.md` (modify — name the reactivate and cancel × webhook race tests)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/domain/subscription.aggregate.ts` (+ spec) (modify — `scheduleCancel`, `clearCancel`, `suspend` reasons)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/controllers/billing.controller.ts` (+ specs), `apps/backend/http/billing/billing.http` (modify)
- `apps/bff/src/features/billing/billing.controller.ts`, `billing.schemas.ts`, `billing.types.ts`, `billing.mapper.ts` (+ specs), `apps/bff/http/billing/billing.http` (modify)
- `packages/types/src/billing.dto.ts` (modify)

**Acceptance criteria — product:**
- [ ] A manager can cancel (access continues to the paid period end), can undo a scheduled cancel, and can reactivate a suspended or cancelled subscription by paying one month; nothing is refunded and no months are owed for the gap.
- [ ] An unpaid trial or a past-due tenant that cancels is suspended at once, with wording that nothing was paid.
- [ ] A suspension by the operator cannot be lifted by the manager.

**Acceptance criteria — technical:**
- Unit:
  - [ ] Cancel per status (A1–A4 and the paid-trial rule); reactivate per reason (A1–A4); recovery amount from today's versions; `anchor_day` clamp to 28
  - [ ] `cancelAt` = `max(period_end)` of `PAID` `PERIOD` charges: with the next period's invoice already `PAID` it is that period's end, not `current_period_end`; a `TRIALING` subscription whose first charge is `PAID` gets `cancelAt` = that first period's end (not `trialEndsAt`)
  - [ ] Error cases: cancel on `SUSPENDED` / `exempt` → `BILLING_CANCEL_NOT_ALLOWED`; reactivate on an `OPERATOR` suspension, on a purged tenant or with nothing to reactivate → `BILLING_REACTIVATE_NOT_ALLOWED`; reactivate with no billing address → `BILLING_ADDRESS_REQUIRED` and no charge created or cancelled
- Integration:
  - [ ] **Concurrency (`UQ_billing_charges_open_recovery`):** two simultaneous reactivates produce one open recovery charge and both responses carry it
  - [ ] **Concurrency (B-R1):** a cancel racing a webhook payment serialises; a payment arriving first leaves the subscription `ACTIVE` with a scheduled cancel only if requested after
  - [ ] Provider cancel failure → local state saved, reconcile later; provider create failure on reactivate → `BILLING_PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE`, charge `PENDING`, retry covered by S05's retry query
  - [ ] "Old charge already paid" path reactivates without a recovery charge
  - [ ] **Cancel then reactivate inside the paid period** with the next period's invoice already issued and cancelled: `clearCancel` re-creates that period's charge (same `period_start`, no `next_due_date` advance) — the period is invoiced exactly once
  - [ ] **Race:** a cancel of a charge whose provider creation is in flight leaves the orphan provider charge marked `provider_cancel_pending` and cancelled by S07's retry
  - [ ] A sweep-held recovery charge (S06) is created at the provider by S05's retry once the address is saved, and a manager `reactivate` meanwhile returns that same open charge (no second recovery charge)
  - [ ] Backend and BFF routes carry `@NoFeatureGate()` + `@SuspensionAccess(MANAGER_BILLING)` (decorator spec)
  - [ ] `seed` now also creates `ACTIVE` with a scheduled cancel and `SUSPENDED` for `CANCELED`, through the normal cancel use case
  - [ ] Setting a tenant `exempt` (operator) cancels its open charge locally and at the provider through `CancelOpenChargesUseCase` (UC-137 step 1)
  - [ ] No provider call inside a transaction (`transactional-io` passes)
  - [ ] Events: `SubscriptionCancelled`/`SubscriptionSuspended`/`ChargeIssued` exactly as documented
- Tenant isolation: [ ] actions affect only the caller's tenant; `STAFF`/`CUSTOMER` → `403`
- E2E: none — covered by integration; the pages' E2E is in S35
- [ ] Coverage ≥80% on changed code
- [ ] `tsc --noEmit` clean, lint clean

### M26-S21 — Chatbot tier → model and caps mapping (Platform)

**Agent:** `backend-ts`
**Complexity:** M
**Docs to load:** `docs/TENANTS_SETTINGS_SCHEMA.md` § chatbot ("Tier source" and the cap fields), `docs/DOMAIN_MODEL.md` § Entry point: `TenantAccessPort` (`features: [{ key: 'CHATBOT', tier }]`), `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_BACKEND.md` § OpenRouter chatbot outbound HTTP resilience (the retry / `provider.ignore` / `reasoning` / `provider.sort` / timeout rules) and § Billing (planned), `docs/USE_CASES.md` — the chatbot **settings** use cases ("Configure chatbot module", UC-027 Section B / UC-026 chatbot section; UC-033 / UC-034 are the guest chatbot use cases), `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_SHARED.md` § Schema-level enforcement, M25-S02 (the validated Plus model and caps)
**Dependencies:** M25-S02 (settled Plus model, caps and OpenRouter validation), M25-S13 (the access answer carries the CHATBOT tier), M25-S17 (the chatbot routes' `@RequiresFeature(CHATBOT)` gates, which this story's "inert" rule complements)
**Pattern:** A pure mapping `chatbotConfigForTier(tier)` in code constants (never in Billing), consumed by the chatbot use cases; the tier read goes through the shared `TenantAccessPort`.

**Description:**
The chatbot becomes a tiered, billed feature (`CHATBOT`, `BASIC` / `PLUS`; display names Básico / Plus). Billing stores only the tier on the subscription item; **Platform maps tier → model + caps in code constants** (`apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/chatbot.constants.ts`, never in Billing). From now on the per-tenant `llmProvider`, `llmModel` and the cap fields are **no longer a manual-override path: they are derived from the tier** — the "direct database update or ad hoc script" path described in the settings Validation Rules is removed by this story, and it **must precede the Plano tab (S30)**. `BASIC` keeps today's code defaults exactly (the `DEFAULT_*` caps in `chatbot.constants.ts` and the current OpenRouter model, which is `DEFAULT_OPENROUTER_MODEL = 'deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash-0731'` inside `infrastructure/llm/openrouter-llm.adapter.ts` — the tier table references it rather than duplicating the id, so `BASIC` stays the adapter's own default), so existing behaviour does not change; `PLUS` is defined by the caps and behaviour M25-S02 validated, so its model can change without repricing (the tier table, `BASIC` and `PLUS` on the same caps with `claude-haiku-5.5` for `PLUS`, is in `docs/TENANTS_SETTINGS_SCHEMA.md` § 7; S21 also decides the spend-breaker value `CHATBOT_GLOBAL_DAILY_SPEND_LIMIT_USD`, a `BASIC` provider price ceiling (`max_price`) and whether a per-tenant monthly bound is needed, as recorded there). A trial tenant gets `BASIC`; a tenant that loses the entitlement (suspended, or `CHATBOT` not in its plan) keeps its `knowledgeText` but the feature is **inert** until entitled again. `knowledgeText` and `maxKnowledgeTextLength` are unaffected. **The Plus `knowledgeText` length is unaffected: `maxKnowledgeTextLength` stays fixed for every tier** (the settings doc says so; M25-S02's mention of a Plus `knowledgeText` length is corrected by its owner), so the tier tables carry only the conversation / message / output-token / history caps and the model. **Fallbacks:** when the access answer has no `CHATBOT` item and the tenant is not entitled → inert; when the access answer fails open (transient lookup error) the tier resolves to `BASIC`, never to an error. Entitlement is already refused at the HTTP choke point (M25-S17 annotates the chatbot routes `@RequiresFeature(CHATBOT)`), so "inert" here means only that `ResolveChatbotConfigUseCase` returns a not-entitled result the use cases honour (no LLM call, no counters touched) for internal callers that bypass the route gate; the public widget sees the typed refusal the gate returns. The `ChatbotSettingsSchema` (in `packages/validation/src/tenant-settings.ts`, not the BFF) keeps rejecting the cap and provider fields (`.strict()`), unchanged. Stored per-tenant `llmProvider` / `llmModel` / cap values are **ignored** after this story (derived values win) — a one-time inventory shows no production tenant has an override (pre-production, none can exist; the story still logs and counts any it finds in local / staging).

**Backend use case steps:**
1. **`ResolveChatbotConfigUseCase`** (tier from the access port → constants), consumed by `SendChatMessageUseCase`, the cap checks (`chatbot-cap-check.helpers.ts`, `get-chatbot-cap-status.use-case.ts`, `get-chatbot-status.use-case.ts`) and the chatbot settings reads.
**Backend HTTP surface:** none new — existing chatbot and settings routes return derived values.
**New migration / i18n keys / env vars / feature flags:** none.

**Files to create/modify:**
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/chatbot.constants.ts` (+ spec) (modify — `CHATBOT_TIER_CONFIG`)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/application/use-cases/resolve-chatbot-config.use-case.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`) (new)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/application/use-cases/chatbot-cap-check.helpers.ts`, `get-chatbot-cap-status.use-case.ts` (+ specs) (modify — the `chatbotSettings.X ?? DEFAULT_X` cap reads; they take the tier-derived values. Only **seven of the eight** `DEFAULT_*` chatbot constants are tier-mapped: `DEFAULT_MAX_KNOWLEDGE_TEXT_LENGTH` stays fixed for every tier. The same `?? DEFAULT_` reads also live in `send-chat-message.use-case.ts` and `get-chatbot-status.use-case.ts`, which take the derived values too), `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/infrastructure/llm/openrouter-llm.adapter.ts` (+ spec) (modify only to export / reference `DEFAULT_OPENROUTER_MODEL` for the `BASIC` entry), `apps/backend/src/shared/value-objects/tenant-settings-data.ts` (+ spec) (modify — the legacy cap / provider fields stay readable but are ignored and counted)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/application/use-cases/send-chat-message.use-case.ts`, `get-chatbot-status.use-case.ts`, `get-tenant-by-id.use-case.ts`, `update-tenant-settings.use-case.ts`, `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/domain/value-objects/tenant-settings.vo.ts`, `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/infrastructure/cross-context/platform-tenant-settings.adapter.ts`, `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/application/services/llm-provider-registry.service.ts` (+ specs) (modify — the verified readers of `llmModel` / `llmProvider`; they take the derived values)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/domain/value-objects/validators/chatbot-settings.validator.ts` (+ spec) (modify)
- `docs/TENANTS_SETTINGS_SCHEMA.md` (modify — the chatbot "Tier source" paragraph loses "planned" once built)

**Acceptance criteria — product:**
- [ ] A Básico tenant's chatbot behaves exactly as today; a Plus tenant gets the Plus model on the same caps as Básico; a tenant without the CHATBOT feature (or suspended) gets an inert chatbot that resumes, with its knowledge text intact, when entitled again.
- [ ] **Recorded at M25-S02, to settle at this story's discovery:** `BASIC`'s measured worst case is about 95% of R$ 39 at the most expensive eligible endpoint, because the adapter's `max_price` (US$ 10 / 50) admits every DeepSeek flash provider (`docs/TENANTS_SETTINGS_SCHEMA.md` § 7, Chatbot Settings). Decide a tier-specific provider price ceiling for `BASIC`: about US$ 0.15 input / 0.50 output per million tokens leaves about 19 endpoints and, at list prices, brings the worst case to about 33% of R$ 39; confirm availability and speed against the live endpoint list before adopting it. This deliberately changes `BASIC`'s routing, so it is the one exception to "behaves exactly as today" above; its model, caps and answers stay the same. Also decide whether `CHATBOT_GLOBAL_DAILY_SPEND_LIMIT_USD` (US$ 1 a day, platform-wide) changes, and whether a per-tenant monthly cost bound is needed, since the daily cap counts new conversations only.

**Acceptance criteria — technical:**
- Unit:
  - [ ] `BASIC` mapping equals today's defaults (**non-regression: snapshot of the current effective values**); `PLUS` equals the M25-S02 values; trial → `BASIC`
  - [ ] Not entitled / suspended → inert (no LLM call, counters untouched); access answer failing open → `BASIC`; `maxKnowledgeTextLength` identical for both tiers
- Integration:
  - [ ] End to end on the fake LLM adapter: tier change takes effect on the next message; stored legacy override fields are ignored and counted
  - [ ] Existing chatbot integration suites pass unchanged — their tenants have no subscription row, so the `InMemoryTenantAccessPort` they use answers an entitled `CHATBOT` item at `BASIC` by default (stated in the double, not per test)
- Tenant isolation: [ ] one tenant's tier never affects another's caps or model
- E2E: none — covered by integration
- [ ] Coverage ≥80% on changed code
- [ ] `tsc --noEmit` clean, lint clean

## Wave 3 — Self-signup and the hotsite module (backend / BFF / packages)

### M26-S22 — Slug rules and availability (UC-128)

**Agent:** `backend-ts + bff-ts`
**Complexity:** M
**Docs to load:** `docs/USE_CASES.md` UC-128 and UC-127 (A1), `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` § Billing — Public Signup API (slug-availability), `docs/BFF_ARCHITECTURE.md` § Module & Controller Naming Conventions (`<module>.public.controller.ts`), `docs/VALUE_OBJECTS_REFERENCE.md` (`Slug`), `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_SHARED.md` § Schema-level enforcement, `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_BACKEND.md` § Billing (planned) (public throttles)
**Dependencies:** none within M26 (uses the existing `Slug` VO and tenant repository)
**Pattern:** A `SlugPolicy` domain service over the `Slug` VO; one shared reserved-word list; a throttled public read.

**Description:**
Today `Slug` is regex-only (`^[a-z0-9-]+$`) and the web proxy reserves only four top-level segments (`RESERVED_TOP_SEGMENTS` in `apps/web/proxy.ts`). Self-signup lets anyone pick a slug, so add the policy. **Rules (from the discovery, `docs/discovery/tenant-billing/tenant-billing.md`):** 3–30 characters, `a-z0-9-`, no leading or trailing hyphen; a **reserved list** made of (a) every real top-level segment of `apps/web/app` (today `api`, `auth`, `bookings`, `dashboard`, `select-staff-tenant`, `switch-tenant`, `v1`), (b) platform words (`ikaro-trial`, `www`, `login`, `account`) and (c) an **impersonation list** (`ikaro`, `admin`, `suporte`, `support`, …); and uniqueness against existing tenants. **Owner exception:** `PLATFORM_OWNER_TENANT_SLUG` (for example `ikaro`) is reserved for the self-signup path but is accepted by the **operator** path (`POST /internal/tenants`, UC-024), so Ikaro's own tenant and the local/staging seeds can still be provisioned; a test pins both directions. The reserved list is a **single source of truth** in `@ikaro/validation` (`packages/validation/src/slug-policy.ts`) used by the backend policy; `apps/web/proxy.ts` keeps its own routing list. **Drift guard:** `apps/web` must never depend on `@ikaro/validation` (`packages/validation/package.json`: "never consumed by apps/web"), so the guard is a spec **inside `packages/validation`** that reads `apps/web/app` with `fs` and asserts every top-level directory (ignoring dynamic `[…]` segments and files) is in the reserved set. `GET /v1/public/signup/slug-availability?slug=` returns `{ status: 'available' }`, `{ status: 'taken' }`, `{ status: 'reserved' }` or `{ status: 'invalid', reason }` (e.g. `TOO_SHORT`), is **throttled at 30 requests per minute per IP** (the existing in-memory BFF throttler, `@Throttle`, best-effort and stated as such; the web's client-side suggestions each re-check, so the limit leaves room) and answers with `Cache-Control: no-store` (a cached `available` would be wrong). It carries no Turnstile (Turnstile is verified only on the final `POST /signup/tenant`, S24; UC-128 A3's Turnstile wording is aligned to the contract — throttled only). **One layer reports each condition** (`docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_SHARED.md` § Schema-level enforcement): the BFF Zod schema checks only the query parameter's shape (present, string); the backend `SlugPolicy` owns `taken` / `reserved` / `invalid` and the codes `SIGNUP_SLUG_INVALID` / `SIGNUP_SLUG_RESERVED` / `SIGNUP_SLUG_TAKEN` (`422` / `422` / `409`, the statuses in `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` § Billing — Public Signup API). The same policy runs again inside the signup use case (S24), so a slug taken between the check and the submit lands in UC-127 A1. Backend: a small `CheckSlugAvailabilityUseCase` in Platform behind an internal endpoint (`TenantRepository.existsBySlug` is not cached, so the answer is live); the BFF route is the public face. The operator provisioning path (UC-024, `POST /internal/tenants`) also applies the policy (a reserved or invalid slug is refused with `422 SIGNUP_SLUG_RESERVED` / `SIGNUP_SLUG_INVALID`, `docs/ERROR_CATALOG.md` § Planned origins: `SIGNUP_*` lists Platform's slug validation on that route) — tested. **Error codes (this story is the first to throw them):** it adds `SIGNUP_SLUG_TAKEN`, `SIGNUP_SLUG_RESERVED`, `SIGNUP_SLUG_INVALID` to `SignupErrorCode`, to both `errors.json` locale files and to the web exhaustiveness spec (M25-S06 translated only the two tax-id codes).

**Backend use case steps:**
1. **`CheckSlugAvailabilityUseCase`**; 2. `SlugPolicy.validate(slug, { allowOwnerSlug })` — a pure function in `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/domain/slug-policy.ts` over the shared list, used by this use case, by S24 and by `ProvisionTenantUseCase`.
**Backend HTTP surface:** new controller `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/infrastructure/controllers/internal-slug.controller.ts` — `GET /internal/signup/slug-availability?slug=` (`InternalApiGuard`; the path is fixed in `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` § Billing — Provider Webhooks and Internal API; deliberately **not** under `/internal/tenants/…`, where a `:tenantId` route would shadow it). Carries `@NoFeatureGate()` and an explicit `@SuspensionAccess(...)` as the M25 route-completeness detector requires (public/tenant-less routes: read M25-S16/S20 for the exact decorator).
**BFF endpoint spec:** `GET /v1/public/signup/slug-availability?slug=` — `@Public()`, `@Throttle` 30/min, `Cache-Control: no-store`; response above. When `FEATURE_FLAG_SIGNUP_ENABLED` is off the route stays readable (S24).
**New migration / i18n keys / env vars / feature flags:** i18n: the three `SIGNUP_SLUG_*` error entries in `packages/i18n/locales/{pt-BR,en}/errors.json`. No migration, env var or flag.

**Files to create/modify:**
- `packages/validation/src/slug-policy.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`, including the drift guard that reads `apps/web/app` with `fs`) (new), `packages/validation/src/index.ts` (modify)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/domain/slug-policy.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`) (new — the `SlugPolicy.validate` function over the shared list)
- `apps/backend/src/shared/value-objects/slug.vo.ts` (+ spec) (modify only if the policy needs a factory hook; keep `create()` regex-only for existing callers)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/application/use-cases/check-slug-availability.use-case.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`, `.integration.spec.ts`) (new)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/application/use-cases/provision-tenant.use-case.ts` (+ spec) (modify — apply the policy; the operator path allows `PLATFORM_OWNER_TENANT_SLUG`)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/infrastructure/controllers/internal-slug.controller.ts` (+ specs), `apps/backend/http/platform/slug.http` (new)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/infrastructure/http/platform-error.mapper.ts` (+ spec) (modify — `SlugPolicy` domain errors to `SIGNUP_SLUG_*`; `error-mapper-coverage`)
- `packages/types/src/error-codes.ts` (modify — `SIGNUP_SLUG_TAKEN`, `SIGNUP_SLUG_RESERVED`, `SIGNUP_SLUG_INVALID`), `packages/i18n/locales/pt-BR/errors.json`, `packages/i18n/locales/en/errors.json` (modify), `apps/web/shared/lib/i18n/error-codes-exhaustiveness.spec.ts` (modify only if the spec needs an entry)
- `apps/bff/src/features/platform/signup.public.controller.ts`, `apps/bff/src/features/platform/signup.public.schemas.ts` (+ specs) (new — BFF naming: public controller, sibling `.schemas.ts`)
- `apps/bff/http/platform/signup.http` (new)
- `apps/bff/src/features/platform/platform.module.ts` (modify)
- `docs/USE_CASES.md` (modify — UC-128 A3 says the slug check is "throttled and Turnstile-protected"; the contract says "throttled" only: the story aligns UC-128 A3 with the contract, i.e. throttled; Turnstile is verified only on the final `POST /signup/tenant`)

**Acceptance criteria — product:**
- [ ] A visitor typing a slug learns immediately whether it is available, taken, reserved or invalid and why; reserved and impersonation words can never be registered, by any path.

**Acceptance criteria — technical:**
- Unit:
  - [ ] Policy table: bounds 3 and 30 (2 and 31 rejected), leading/trailing hyphen, every reserved class (web top-level routes including `bookings`, platform words, impersonation list), invalid characters, case
  - [ ] Owner slug: reserved for the signup path, accepted by the operator path
  - [ ] Drift guard (in `packages/validation`): every `apps/web/app` top-level directory is reserved
  - [ ] Throttle decorator present on the route (30/min) and `Cache-Control: no-store` set
- Integration:
  - [ ] Availability against real tenants (taken) and against a slug taken during the call (the signup path catches it, tested in S24)
  - [ ] `POST /internal/tenants` with a reserved slug is refused with `422 SIGNUP_SLUG_RESERVED`; with the owner slug it succeeds
- Tenant isolation: none for the public read (it returns a status only, never tenant data) — asserted: the response carries no tenant fields
- E2E: none — covered by integration; the page E2E is S35
- [ ] Coverage ≥80% on changed code
- [ ] `tsc --noEmit` clean, lint clean

### M26-S23 — Public prices (UC-128)

**Agent:** `backend-ts + bff-ts`
**Complexity:** S
**Docs to load:** `docs/USE_CASES.md` UC-128 (A1), `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` § Billing — Public Signup API (prices), `docs/DOMAIN_MODEL.md` § Aggregate: FeaturePrice (versions, `upcoming`), `docs/DATABASE_SCHEMA.md` § `billing.feature_prices`
**Dependencies:** M25-S04 (`ListSelectablePricesUseCase`, price versions), M25-S13 (creates the BFF `features/billing/billing.module.ts` this story extends)
**Pattern:** Cacheable read model; the exempt-from-`tenant_id` aggregate.

**Description:**
`GET /v1/public/billing/prices?country=BR` returns `{ countryCode, currency, taxBehavior: 'INCLUSIVE', items: [{ featureKey, tier, amountMinor, upcoming?: { amountMinor, effectiveFrom } }] }` — the selectable list prices of the country (hidden prices excluded), with `upcoming` only when a dated change is scheduled. Feature descriptions are translated copy in the web app, not API data. The route is `@Public()`, **cacheable**, reads through the M25-S04 use case via an internal endpoint, and carries no tenant data. **Cache:** the BFF sends `Cache-Control: public, max-age=300` (the manifest precedent in `platform.public.controller.ts`); the response varies by `country`, so the query string is part of the cache key, and a price that becomes effective is visible within five minutes. **"Current" date:** the route has no tenant, so "now" is the calendar date in `America/Sao_Paulo` for BR (the CountrySpec default timezone of the requested country), the same date basis the price-in-force lookup uses for a service-period start. **`upcoming`:** at most one per item — the earliest version with `effective_from` after today whose `announced_at IS NOT NULL` and `superseded_at IS NULL` (a scheduled-but-not-announced version never leaks). A country with no selectable prices, or an unsupported `country`, returns `200` with `items: []` (the page treats it as "not offered"); a malformed `country` value is `400`. The US must not be offered before M28 (UC-127 A7), but its seed rows exist in every environment since M25-S04, so `?country=US` returns its prices everywhere and the absence of prices can no longer gate the option: **decide at this story's discovery how a country is enabled for signup** (an explicit supported-country list, or the US rows seeded `selectable = false` until M28), and make the signup country selector, UC-127 A7 and the price page follow that one rule. A price-load failure on the page leaves signup possible (UC-128 A1, handled in S33). The route stays readable when `FEATURE_FLAG_SIGNUP_ENABLED` is off (S24).

**Backend use case steps:** reuses `ListSelectablePricesUseCase` (M25-S04), adding the `upcoming` projection.
**Backend HTTP surface:** new `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/controllers/internal-billing-prices.controller.ts` — `GET /internal/billing/public-prices?country=` (`InternalApiGuard`; `@NoFeatureGate()` and the explicit `@SuspensionAccess(...)` the M25 detector requires for a tenant-less route).
**BFF endpoint spec:** `GET /v1/public/billing/prices?country=` — `@Public()`, `Cache-Control: public, max-age=300`; response above (enums and amounts only, no display strings).
**New migration / i18n keys / env vars / feature flags:** none.

**Files to create/modify:**
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/application/use-cases/list-selectable-prices.use-case.ts` (+ spec) (modify — `upcoming`)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/controllers/internal-billing-prices.controller.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`, `.integration.spec.ts`) (new), `apps/backend/http/billing/prices.http` (new)
- `apps/bff/src/features/billing/billing.public.controller.ts`, `billing.public.schemas.ts` (+ specs) (new), `apps/bff/src/features/billing/billing.module.ts` (modify), `apps/bff/http/billing/prices.http` (new)
- `packages/types/src/billing.dto.ts` (modify)

**Acceptance criteria — product:**
- [ ] The public page can show the current price table for Brazil, including an announced increase with its date; hidden prices never appear.

**Acceptance criteria — technical:**
- Unit: [ ] projection with and without a scheduled version; hidden excluded; two future versions → only the earliest announced one; an unannounced future version never appears; cache headers set; US returns `items: []` while the flag is off
- Integration: [ ] real seeded prices returned for BR; a future version appears only as `upcoming`; unsupported country → `200 { items: [] }`, malformed → `400`
- Tenant isolation: none — platform-wide data with no tenant owner (the documented `feature_prices` exemption)
- E2E: none — covered by integration
- [ ] Coverage ≥80% on changed code
- [ ] `tsc --noEmit` clean, lint clean

### M26-S24 — Self-signup — session, trial rule, signup use case and BFF routes (UC-127)

**Agent:** `bff-ts + backend-ts + devops`
**Complexity:** L
**Docs to load:** `docs/USE_CASES.md` UC-127 (all, A1–A10), UC-110 and UC-024, `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` § Billing — Public Signup API (BFF, no JWT) and § Billing — Provider Webhooks and Internal API (the registrations route and the `POST /internal/tenants` row), `docs/LOCK_CATALOG.md` § Billing locks (B-A1), `docs/DOMAIN_EVENTS.md` § TenantProvisioned, `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_FRONTEND.md` § CSP allowances for a new external UI resource and `docs/CI_TRAPS.md` § Cloudflare Turnstile's test sitekey, `docs/BFF_ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_BACKEND.md` § Billing (planned), `journeys/guest/ikaro-trial.md`
**Dependencies:** M26-S22 (slug policy), M26-S08 (the one-hour `RESERVED` release is the backstop), M25-S06 (`TaxId`, tax-id error codes), M25-S09 (registration reservation, B-A1), M25-S10 (provisioning with `registrationId`), M25-S11 (the trial handler), M25-S13 (tenant-info port, for `existingTenantSlug`)
**Pattern:** Orchestration in the BFF over internal endpoints (Billing reserves and releases, Platform provisions); a short-lived signed cookie signed with the existing `JwtService`; the existing Turnstile verifier port; B-A1 exercised end to end.

**Description:**
Anyone can start a free trial. **Session (Google step).** The visitor signs in with Google through the existing OAuth flow with a new `type=signup` branch (`GET /auth/google?type=signup`): no Turnstile on this step (Turnstile is verified only on the final `POST /signup/tenant`). The callback checks the Google profile's **email-verified flag** (the existing strategy reads only `emails[0].value`; a Google account whose email is unverified is refused with the "Google account unverified" error page, because the email becomes the first manager), then keeps only `{ email, name, googleSub }` in a **short-lived signed cookie**: signed with the existing `JwtService` (`JWT_SECRET`, the `OAuthStateService` precedent — **no new signing secret and no new devops half**) with a distinct `typ`/`aud` claim (`signup-session`) so it can never be accepted as a session JWT or an OAuth state, a distinct cookie name (`__Host-signup_session` in production, `signup_session` elsewhere, mirroring `SESSION_COOKIE_NAME`), `httpOnly`, `Secure` outside local, `SameSite=Lax`, `Path=/`, 30 minutes. No tenant exists yet and **no tenant JWT is ever issued** (there is no existing "staff selection token" to reuse). The callback redirects to the `/ikaro-trial/entrar` page (S33 owns the form step on that route); that redirect is set by the signup branch itself, outside the slug-bound `isValidReturnTo` path (see the verified-files list below for the guard / state-encoder changes this branch needs). A **dev/test signup-session issuer** (`POST /auth/dev-signup-session`, `{ email, name, googleSub }`, gated exactly like `dev-login` by `ENABLE_DEV_AUTH` and refused when `APP_ENV=production`) sets the same cookie so Playwright (S33, S35) can obtain a signup session without Google.

**Read.** `GET /v1/public/signup/session` returns `{ email, name, trialAvailable, existingTenantSlug?, termsVersion }`. `trialAvailable` and `existingTenantSlug` come from a **read-only** Billing eligibility query (`GET /internal/billing/registrations/eligibility?googleSub=`, new here — the tax id is checked on the final `POST` only, never on this read: the same rule as M25-S09 but with no lock and no insert; `POST /internal/billing/registrations` inserts a `RESERVED` row and must not be called from a read). `existingTenantSlug` is the slug of the earliest unpaid self-signup tenant of that `sub`, resolved through the M25-S13 tenant-info port. `termsVersion` is the backend env `SIGNUP_TERMS_VERSION` — the **single source**: the web renders the hosted text for that version and sends it back as `acceptedTermsVersion`; the web keeps no duplicate constant (S33). Without a valid cookie the route answers `401` (confirm at story discovery). The session read does not know the tax id yet, so the tax-id half of the trial rule is applied at reservation (below), and the page learns of it from `409 SIGNUP_TRIAL_NOT_AVAILABLE` on submit.

**Trial rule.** A signup is refused with `409 SIGNUP_TRIAL_NOT_AVAILABLE` (A3) when (a) an earlier self-signup tenant of that Google `sub` never paid (M25-S09), or (b) the **normalised CPF/CNPJ already belongs to a `billing_accounts` row whose subscription has never paid and is not purged** (trial-farming rule). (b) compares the stored digits-only `billing_accounts.tax_id` directly (`docs/DATABASE_SCHEMA.md` L1300) through a standalone index on it — see **Notes for other owners** below; both checks run inside the same B-A1 transaction, and the cross-Google-account tax-id check is best-effort under concurrency (B-A1 serialises per `sub`, not per tax id) — stated, not hidden (`docs/LOCK_CATALOG.md` states the same best-effort caveat next to B-A1; the docs editor adds it). Eligibility is released only by M27 discard or the operator offboarding runbook (M17-S51), never automatically (UC-127 A3).

**Create.** `POST /v1/public/signup/tenant` `{ businessName, slug, countryCode: 'BR', taxId (BR), acceptedTermsVersion, turnstileToken }`; email and name come **from the session, never from the body**; the language derives from `countryCode` (no language field); `countryCode` is `BR` only until M28 (a US request is `422` with the A7 "not offered" mapping; confirm the exact code at discovery). The order is fixed so a recoverable error never costs the visitor their trial:
1. **Stateless validations first, nothing persisted:** the BFF Zod shape check, then Platform's `ValidateSignupUseCase` through the new stateless `POST /internal/signup/validate` (decided by the editor: a separate route, so reservation can sit between validation and provisioning without a `phase` flag) verifies Turnstile **once** (the only Turnstile verification in the flow — `POST /internal/signup/tenant` does not verify it again; the existing `ITurnstileVerifierPort`, token single-use, `clientIp` forwarded as the lead form does, BFF → backend timeout 15 s like `LEAD_FORM_SUBMISSION_TIMEOUT_MS`), the slug policy (S22) and availability (non-binding), the terms version against the current `SIGNUP_TERMS_VERSION` (`SIGNUP_TERMS_NOT_ACCEPTED`, A5) and the tax id (`SIGNUP_TAX_ID_REQUIRED` / `SIGNUP_TAX_ID_INVALID`, A9).
2. **Reserve:** the BFF calls Billing `POST /internal/billing/registrations` (B-A1 lock on the Google `sub`, the trial rule above → `409 SIGNUP_TRIAL_NOT_AVAILABLE`), storing the Google `sub`, the BR tax id and the accepted terms version and timestamp (set by the BFF at acceptance) in `signup_registrations`.
3. **Provision:** the BFF calls `POST /internal/signup/tenant` with the `registrationId` (required); `SignupTenantUseCase` re-applies the cheap stateless checks (slug policy, terms version, tax id) but does **not** consume a second Turnstile token (verified in step 1), and calls the **existing** `ProvisionTenantUseCase` with the Google email as first manager and the `registrationId`. A slug taken between the check and the commit surfaces as a unique violation in `ProvisionTenantUseCase` (it does `existsBySlug` then `save`; `tenants.slug` is `unique`), which that use case now maps to `SlugAlreadyTakenError`; `SignupTenantUseCase` maps it to `409 SIGNUP_SLUG_TAKEN` (A1).
4. **On ANY failure after step 2** (including a network error), the BFF calls the idempotent `POST /internal/billing/registrations/{id}/release` in a `finally`, deleting the `RESERVED` row; the one-hour sweep in S08 (step 1b) is only the backstop. **Acceptance: a retry after A1 / A2 / A5 / A9 / A6 succeeds immediately.**

**Transaction and network I/O placement:** the Turnstile verification (a network call) and the BFF → Billing / BFF → Platform calls sit in separate use cases and **never inside `txManager.run()`**; the Billing reservation and Platform provisioning are two separate transactions. None of the billing identity data (Google `sub`, tax id, terms) travels in the event (`TenantProvisioned` carries only `countryCode`, `timezone` and `registrationId` as billing references, next to the tenant's `name`, `slug` and `adminEmail`). `201 { tenantSlug, nextPath: '/ikaro-trial/conta-criada' }`; the signup-session cookie is cleared and replaced by the **resend credential** cookie (a signed value bound to `{ tenantId, email }`, 30 minutes, same attributes and `JwtService`, `typ`/`aud` `signup-resend`, which S25 consumes); the first-manager invitation / login email follows asynchronously from `TenantProvisioned`. **Failures:** Turnstile fails → `400 SIGNUP_TURNSTILE_FAILED` (A2); rate limit exceeded → `429 SIGNUP_RATE_LIMITED` with `Retry-After` (A4); `FEATURE_FLAG_SIGNUP_ENABLED` off → `503 SIGNUP_CLOSED` on the session read, the Google step and both `POST`s (slug availability and prices stay readable; the real "stop signups" lever, drilled in S39); US selected or a country without prices → not offered (A7); tenant-creation fails → nothing is created and a retry is offered (A6 — the durable event retry completes a delayed `TenantProvisioned` consumer; the self-heal job S08 repairs a missing trial); a Google account that already manages other tenants → allowed, trial rule still applies (A8).

**Design notes carried from M25-S10 (2026-11-11; settle them at this story's `/story-discovery`, nothing here is built).** (1) *One provisioning pattern.* Operator and self-signup both reserve a Billing registration first and create the tenant with its `registrationId` (M25-S10 made the id required in `ProvisionTenantUseCase`, so `SignupTenantUseCase` always passes it). Platform holds no tax id, no exempt flag and no feature list; the registration is the single holder of the billing intent. (2) *Why `validate` stays a separate first call.* It verifies Turnstile **before** the reservation, so a bot cannot spam `POST /internal/billing/registrations` (a lock on the Google `sub` plus a row) without being checked; the single-use token is verified once, there. Do not fold it into the provisioning call without moving the bot check elsewhere. (3) *The tax id is validated once, at the reservation* (Billing, `TaxId`); the `validate` pre-check and `SignupTenantUseCase` re-check are early-refusal conveniences for the form, not a second source of truth, so keep them to the shape check or drop them. (4) *Open product question for the owner:* must an ineligible visitor be refused before any tenant exists (UC-127 today: yes, hence reserve-before-create, the release call and the one-hour sweep)? A tenant-first alternative (create the tenant, let Billing's handler decide the trial) removes the reservation role for self-signup but changes that rule; it would also need another route for the tax id into Billing that is not the event.

**Rate limits (numbers; stated honestly).** Signup attempts: **5 per IP per day** (best-effort — the existing in-memory BFF throttler, per instance; not a hard guarantee); **3 per Google account per day** (durable, counted from `signup_registrations` rows of that `sub` created in the last 24 h, so no new table or migration). The hard anti-abuse controls are Turnstile, the per-`sub` B-A1 trial rule and the per-tax-id rule. The throttler override on the signup routes emits `429 SIGNUP_RATE_LIMITED` with `Retry-After` (the global `AppThrottlerGuard` throws `AUTH_RATE_LIMITED` with no header and is skipped unless `APP_ENV` is `staging`/`production`); the override is a route-level guard that is **testable under `APP_ENV=local`** by taking the throttle's enabled flag from an injectable provider the test sets. The signup routes are platform-level and carry no tenant slug header; `@BypassWebOnlyGuard()` exists (`apps/bff/src/shared/decorators/bypass-web-only-guard.decorator.ts`; S04 uses it for the provider webhook), but the signup routes need no bypass because ikaro-web calls them server-side with its web key. The `/v1` web gateway has an 8 s timeout (`BFF_GATEWAY_TIMEOUT_MS`): the signup `POST` goes through the Next server-side fetcher (`bffServerFetch`-style, S33), not the gateway, because the Turnstile verification can take close to 8 s on its own.

**Backend use case steps:**
1. **`ValidateSignupUseCase`** (Platform): Turnstile, slug policy and availability, terms version, tax id — persists nothing. 2. **`SignupTenantUseCase`** (Platform): re-applies the cheap checks, provisions with the required `registrationId` through the existing `ProvisionTenantUseCase`, maps the slug race. 3. **`GetSignupEligibilityUseCase`** (Billing): the read-only eligibility query (no lock, no insert). 4. **`ReleaseSignupRegistrationUseCase`** (Billing): deletes a `RESERVED` row, idempotent, refuses a `COMPLETED` one. 5. BFF orchestration: session read → validate → reserve → provision → on any failure after reserve, release (`finally`) → set the resend credential and clear the session cookie.
**Backend HTTP surface:**
- new `apps/backend/src/contexts/platform/infrastructure/controllers/internal-signup.controller.ts` — `POST /internal/signup/validate` (new here) and `POST /internal/signup/tenant` (path fixed in `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md`), both `InternalApiGuard` alone.
- extends the Billing registration controller (M25-S09, `internal-billing-registration.controller.ts`) — `GET /internal/billing/registrations/eligibility?googleSub=` and `POST /internal/billing/registrations/{id}/release`; `POST /internal/billing/registrations` is reused.
- Every new route carries `@NoFeatureGate()` and the explicit `@SuspensionAccess(...)` the M25 route-completeness detector requires for tenant-less internal routes (read M25-S16/S20/S24 for the exact decorator).
**BFF endpoint spec:** extends `apps/bff/src/features/platform/signup.public.controller.ts` — `GET /v1/public/signup/session`, `POST /v1/public/signup/tenant` (`@Public()`, throttles as above, `503 SIGNUP_CLOSED` when `FEATURE_FLAG_SIGNUP_ENABLED` is off); the cookie service in `signup-session.service.ts`; `GET /auth/google?type=signup` and the callback branch in the auth feature; `POST /auth/dev-signup-session` (dev/test only).
**New migration / i18n keys / env vars / feature flags:**
- env: the current Terms version `SIGNUP_TERMS_VERSION` (backend only; added to backend `env.validation.ts` and the env contract; required when `FEATURE_FLAG_SIGNUP_ENABLED` is true) and the flag `FEATURE_FLAG_SIGNUP_ENABLED` (default `false` in production until S39, `true` in local/CI/staging; backend and BFF read it). `TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY` is the existing backend variable the lead form already uses. **No new secret.**
- i18n (this story first throws these codes, M25-S06 translated only the two tax-id codes `SIGNUP_TAX_ID_INVALID` and `SIGNUP_TAX_ID_REQUIRED`): `SIGNUP_TERMS_NOT_ACCEPTED`, `SIGNUP_TRIAL_NOT_AVAILABLE`, `SIGNUP_RATE_LIMITED`, `SIGNUP_TURNSTILE_FAILED`, `SIGNUP_CLOSED` added to `SignupErrorCode`, both `errors.json` locale files and the web exhaustiveness spec; the two BFF-thrown ones (`SIGNUP_RATE_LIMITED`, `SIGNUP_CLOSED`) also to `BffThrowableCode`; `SIGNUP_TURNSTILE_FAILED` is thrown by the backend `ValidateSignupUseCase` (it passes through the BFF unchanged, so it is not a `BffThrowableCode`). Default HTTP statuses (confirm at story discovery): `SIGNUP_TURNSTILE_FAILED` 400, `SIGNUP_TRIAL_NOT_AVAILABLE` 409, `SIGNUP_TERMS_NOT_ACCEPTED` 422, `SIGNUP_RATE_LIMITED` 429, `SIGNUP_CLOSED` 503.
- migration: none for the signup flow; the tax-id lookup index is owned by M25-S03 (see **Notes for other owners**).

**Notes for other owners (canonical docs / M25, not edited here):** (1) M25-S03 / `docs/DATABASE_SCHEMA.md`: the standalone partial index on `billing.billing_accounts (tax_id) WHERE tax_id IS NOT NULL` (cross-tenant, no `tenant_id` available at signup) is already documented as `IDX_billing_billing_accounts_tax_id`; a standalone index on `signup_registrations (created_at)` is not needed (the per-`sub` daily count uses the existing `google_sub` index). (2) `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` § Billing — Public Signup API and § Provider Webhooks and Internal API: `POST /internal/signup/validate` (the row is added by the docs editor in the audit fix; the validate route is the only Turnstile verification), the eligibility and release routes, `termsVersion` on the session response, `SIGNUP_CLOSED`, `countryCode` `BR` only until M28, Turnstile only on the final `POST`, the throttle numbers. (3) `docs/USE_CASES.md` UC-127: Turnstile placement, A3 release is not automatic, the tax-id rule, the kill switch; `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_INFRA.md` env list: `FEATURE_FLAG_SIGNUP_ENABLED`, `SIGNUP_TERMS_VERSION`. (4) M25-S09 L612 limitation reworded: M26-S24 releases the reservation on failure.

**Devops half (env only, no secret).** `SIGNUP_TERMS_VERSION` and `FEATURE_FLAG_SIGNUP_ENABLED` are plain env vars: `env_vars` in `infra/terraform/envs/staging/main.tf` and `envs/prod/main.tf` (prod: `FEATURE_FLAG_SIGNUP_ENABLED=false` and a placeholder terms version until S39), the env contract (`exemptKeys` for the local defaults). **PR sequence:** 1 PR (no new secret, topic or subscription). **Live-verification check:** after apply on staging, `gcloud run services describe <backend>` shows both vars; a smoke `GET /v1/public/signup/session` without a cookie returns the documented unauthenticated answer (and `503 SIGNUP_CLOSED` once the flag is flipped off in a throwaway revision). Pass = all three.
