### M26-S36 — Alert policies, KPIs and as-built runbooks

**Agent:** `devops + backend-ts`
**Complexity:** M
**Docs to load:** `docs/OBSERVABILITY_STRATEGY.md` § 7. Dashboards, alerts and uptime checks (the Billing alert policies and KPI paragraph) and § 4 Metrics, `docs/RUNBOOKS.md` § Billing (planned) and § Simulate an incident (verify alerts), `infra/terraform/README.md` § New-resource PR-sequencing playbook and `infra/terraform/modules/monitoring/`, `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_INFRA.md` § Billing configuration (planned), `packages/observability/src/**`
**Dependencies:** M26-S04 (webhook metrics, dispute-opened log field), M26-S05 (stuck-creation signal), M26-S06 (sweep log fields it modifies), M26-S07 (drift, reconcile and `billing_kpi_daily` signals), M26-S08 (freeze, missing subscription), M26-S09 and M26-S10 (job log fields it modifies), M26-S12 (the `billing_template_missing` signal), M25-S15a (`billing.operator_audit`, source of the intervention KPI)
**Pattern:** Log-based metrics + alert policies in the existing `modules/monitoring/` (module variables for thresholds); each alert names its runbook.

**Description:**
Billing is operator-free by default: humans are paged only after the automatic response failed. **Log-based metrics only** — no application-defined OTel instrument is added (`docs/OBSERVABILITY_STRATEGY.md` §4: "No application code defines its own OTel instruments"; an SDK metric needs a matching collector pipeline), and `packages/observability` gains no counter. Each metric reads a named structured log message and field that the job stories emit; where one is missing S36 adds it (the six job use cases below). The webhook signal is a pair of log-based metrics over the BFF's webhook-route request logs (Cloud Run's `request_count` has no path label, so the existing 5xx policy cannot be reused): `billing_webhook_failure` (non-2xx other than `401`) and `billing_webhook_ok`.

Alert policies (thresholds are module variables, with these defaults, all **count-based** because webhook volume is a handful per day and a percentage over a tiny denominator is noise):
- **Webhook failure:** ≥ 3 consecutive failures, or any `5xx`, on the webhook route within 15 min, or the provider queue paused and no delivery has succeeded since it was re-enabled (S08 `provider_health` log). A BFF `401` (wrong or rotated token, scanners) is **excluded** and never pages: it is not a processing failure and a token rotation produces a short burst by design (Asaas documents a pause after 15 consecutive failures, not reproduced in the sandbox (`docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md` G3), which the "queue paused" leg covers; that leg stays raised until a delivery succeeds after the re-enable, so a wrong token that pauses the queue again keeps paging, `docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md` § 4).
- **Reconcile failing:** 2 consecutive daily-pass failures or 24 h without a success (the suspension freeze starts at the same 24 h).
- **Reconcile drift:** any daily-pass charge difference (warning) and more than 5 in one run (critical).
- **Charge creation stuck:** a charge `PENDING` provider creation with ≥ 3 attempts or older than 1 h.
- **Missing subscription:** a tenant older than 30 min with none (self-heal could not create it).
- **Suspension freeze active:** self-heal logs the freeze state on every hourly run while it is set; the policy fires when the metric is > 0 in the last hour.
- **Missing platform template:** S11 / S12 emit `billing_template_missing`; one occurrence fires (billing emails are money-critical and a template can be absent at runtime for one locale).
- **Dispute opened:** S04 logs a structured `billing_dispute_opened` line for a chargeback request / dispute (no access change, no new table); one occurrence fires, so an operator can upload evidence in the provider console before its deadline.
- **Billing job did not run:** for the issue, sweep, reminders and reconcile jobs (all six jobs are created paused in production until S39), a log-absence condition on each job's per-run log line (sweep: 2 h, the daily jobs: 26 h).

The *purge step stuck* policy is M27. A missing price row and a live-adapter misconfiguration have **no alert policy** by design (CI / seed-consistency test and startup catch a missing price row; a wrong merchant profile key throws on lookup and surfaces as an ordinary backend error).

**Weekly KPIs, defined (numbers on the dashboard, never a worklist).** The reconcile **daily pass** (S07) emits one structured line `billing_kpi_daily` with counts; the dashboard widgets read it with log-based metrics, and the one KPI that does not come from it comes from `billing.operator_audit`:
- *share of charges paid with no manual action* = charges that became `PAID` in the period via webhook / reconcile ÷ charges that became `PAID` (an operator never marks a charge paid, so this is a drift indicator: PAID-by-reconcile ÷ PAID);
- *involuntary churn* = subscriptions suspended for `NON_PAYMENT` in the period and still `SUSPENDED` at its end ÷ active at its start;
- *webhook processing lag* = `received_at → processed_at` of `provider_events` (the table stores no provider timestamp, so a payment-to-webhook lag is **not** measurable and is dropped);
- *reconcile corrections per run* = the daily pass's corrected-charge count;
- *time from payment to reactivation* = `paid_at` → the `SubscriptionReactivated` time for reactivating payments;
- *open exceptions older than 24 h* = `PENDING` provider creations, `provider_cancel_pending` charges and held recovery charges older than 24 h;
- *operator interventions per 100 active tenants per month* = `billing.operator_audit` rows per month ÷ active subscriptions × 100, emitted by the same daily line (a count read through the audit repository), so no BigQuery or SQL dashboard is needed.

`docs/RUNBOOKS.md` § Billing (planned) is updated to **as-built**: every alert's runbook (Webhook, Provider incident, Drift, Charge creation, Provisioning, Dispute, Job-did-not-run, Catalog) and the mapped operator cases (suspend / unsuspend / grace, exempt, **chargeback**, refunds, LGPD requests, secret rotation, the interim offboarding entry for never-paid tenants until M27), using real commands (`gcloud scheduler jobs run ikaro-cron-billing-<name>`, the operator routes). Three entries change from the planned text: (1) **chargeback** — until the Asaas event that finalizes a lost dispute is verified (S37, S39), chargeback-driven `PAYMENT_REVERSED` suspension is **off**; an operator who learns a dispute was lost suspends the tenant manually (`OPERATOR` reason) and uploads evidence in the provider console; (2) **refund trap** — a **full** refund of the current period triggers the `PAYMENT_REVERSED` suspension, so the safe order is: prefer a partial refund; if a full refund is required, mark the tenant exempt — after `PAYMENT_REVERSED` the operator unsuspend is **not** allowed (`BILLING_UNSUSPEND_NOT_ALLOWED`), so exempt is the way back; a full refund of the **first** invoice while `TRIALING` suspends nothing (S04), the charge stays `REFUNDED` and is treated as unpaid at trial end, so a recovery charge is issued then (S06 rule 2); (3) **secret rotation** — the webhook token in S01's order, plus the **GitHub repository copy** of the sandbox key that S37's nightly job uses. Cancelling an NFS-e after a refund / chargeback is the accountant's procedure recorded in S39. A new log-based metric can take up to 10 minutes to become queryable; the module's `time_sleep.log_metric_propagation` `triggers` map gains each new metric the alert policies reference.

**PR sequence:** 2 PRs, because the story spans `infra/terraform/**` and `apps/**` and CI's `no-infra-app-mix` job (`.github/workflows/pr-quality.yml`) rejects a mixed PR without the `infra-app-mix-ok` label: PR1 (app + docs) — the structured log fields / lines the job stories must emit (`billing_kpi_daily`, `billing_dispute_opened`, per-run job lines, freeze state) + `docs/RUNBOOKS.md` + `docs/OBSERVABILITY_STRATEGY.md`; PR2 (devops) — metric / alert definitions in `modules/monitoring` + envs thresholds, merged after PR1 so the log messages exist. (If the owner prefers one PR, it carries `infra-app-mix-ok` with the apply-first note.) Apply to staging and trigger **each** condition (see the check below) before trusting it; no new topic and no new secret.
**IAM / permissions:** none beyond the monitoring module's existing grants.
**Live-verification check:** on staging after apply, using `docs/RUNBOOKS.md` § Simulate an incident (verify alerts) as the base procedure. That section today only describes stopping Cloud SQL, which trips unrelated alerts; S36 therefore **adds a webhook-specific step** to it (an authenticated `POST` to the staging webhook route with the real `ASAAS_WEBHOOK_TOKEN`, read with the operator's own identity, while the backend cannot process it, for example with Cloud SQL stopped for a few minutes) and uses that step here. (1) Webhook failure fires and emails the `notification_email` channel, recovers, and a burst of wrong-token `401`s does **not** fire it; (2) missing template: trigger one missing-template failure (a throwaway locale / key) and confirm the alert; (3) reconcile failing / job-did-not-run: pause the staging reconcile scheduler job past the threshold (the freeze is global per provider, not per tenant: pause it on staging only and resume afterwards) and confirm both alerts and that `freeze active` is observable; (4) drift, creation-stuck, missing-subscription, dispute-opened: trigger each once on staging through the fake / seed paths (a drifted charge via `simulate` DELAYED, a held creation via the fake's `PROVIDER_5XX`, a subscription row deleted before self-heal, a `CHARGEBACK_OPENED` simulate) — or, for any alert that cannot be triggered live, state it in the PR as proven by the unit / terraform test only; (5) every new metric is queryable (`gcloud logging metrics list`). Pass = each alert observed firing (and recovering where it can), silent on the `401` burst, metrics listed, and the runbook link in each alert body resolves.

**Backend use case steps:** none.
**Backend HTTP surface:** none.
**New migration / i18n keys / env vars / feature flags:** none.

**Files to create/modify:**
- `infra/terraform/modules/monitoring/main.tf`, `infra/terraform/modules/monitoring/variables.tf`, `infra/terraform/modules/monitoring/tests/resources_unit_test.tftest.hcl`, `infra/terraform/modules/monitoring/tests/variables_unit_test.tftest.hcl` (modify), `infra/terraform/envs/staging/main.tf`, `infra/terraform/envs/prod/main.tf` (modify — thresholds, only if non-default)
- the job use cases created in S05–S10 (modify — `tenant_id`, counts and correlation fields added to their log lines): `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/application/use-cases/issue-next-charges.use-case.ts`, `sweep-subscription-statuses.use-case.ts`, `reconcile-charges.use-case.ts`, `self-heal.use-case.ts`, `publish-billing-reminders.use-case.ts`, `purge-provider-events.use-case.ts`
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/application/use-cases/process-provider-webhook.use-case.ts` (modify — the structured `billing_dispute_opened` line; S04 owns the file)
- `docs/RUNBOOKS.md`, `docs/OBSERVABILITY_STRATEGY.md` (modify — as-built; log-based only; the alert list above; the webhook-failure policy excludes `401`; the missing-template policy is kept; the KPI definitions and the `billing_kpi_daily` line; the webhook-specific simulate-incident step)

**Acceptance criteria — product:**
- [ ] An operator is alerted only when an automatic response has failed, with a link to the exact runbook; the weekly numbers are visible without a worklist.

**Acceptance criteria — technical:**
- Unit: [ ] each job use case emits its per-run line and the structured fields the metrics read (`billing_kpi_daily`, `billing_dispute_opened`, freeze state); the `terraform test` suite for the module passes, including the new `time_sleep` triggers
- Integration: none
- Tenant isolation: none — platform operational metrics (no tenant data in alert bodies beyond ids)
- E2E: none
- [ ] Checkov / Tfsec clean
- [ ] Coverage ≥80% on changed code
- [ ] `tsc --noEmit` clean, lint clean

### M26-S37 — Real `AsaasBillingProvider`, sandbox contract run and the nightly job **[GATE]**

**Agent:** `backend-ts + devops`
**Complexity:** L
**Docs to load:** `docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md` (the verified Asaas facts, written by M25-S01), `docs/TECH_STACK_DECISIONS.md` § 10. PAYMENT PROVIDERS, `docs/TESTING_STRATEGY.md` § Testing the billing provider — fake, sandbox, contract suite and clock, `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_TESTING.md` § Billing provider tests (planned), `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_INFRA.md` § Billing configuration (planned), `docs/CI_CD_PIPELINE.md` § Nightly billing provider contract job, `docs/GITHUB_WORKFLOWS_GUIDELINES.md`, `docs/CI_TRAPS.md`, `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_BACKEND.md` § OpenRouter chatbot outbound HTTP resilience (the undici retry / timeout traps apply to any outbound HTTP client)
**Dependencies:** M25-S01 (verified provider facts), M25-S14 (port, fake, contract suite, config guard, `BillingProviderRegistry`), M26-S01 (secrets and env), M26-S04 (the webhook that normalises Asaas events), M26-S05 (the issue job the staging live check drives), M26-S07 (reconcile, the second consumer of `fetchChargeStatus`)
**Pattern:** Adapter implementing `BillingProviderPort` (Strategy-per-provider); the **same contract suite** runs against both implementations; a scheduled GitHub workflow runs the sandbox leg.

**Description:**
Replace the fake in production with the real thing, and keep the fake honest. **`AsaasBillingProvider`** implements the port against Asaas: `ensureCustomer` (create **or update**: `name` + `cpfCnpj` required, `notificationDisabled = true`, **our tenant / account id as the customer `externalReference`**, and the billing identity's **email and address (street, number, complement, neighborhood, `postalCode`/CEP, city, state)** because provider-automatic NFS-e needs the payer's full data — `docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md` A15; an identity that changed since the customer was created updates the existing Asaas customer, S16 syncs it at the next issue), `createCharge` (`billingType = UNDEFINED`, our charge id as `externalReference`, hosted `invoiceUrl`, **explicit zero interest and fine and no late-fee field** — `docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md` A5: the tenant owes the list price only — amount sent as the decimal-real value converted from `MinorAmount`, and the `returnUrl` mapped to Asaas's `callback` where the account's domain rule allows it, M26-D10 (observed: the sandbox refuses a `callback` with 400 `invalid_object` until a site is registered on the account, `docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md` A25); both are marked *unverified* below), `regeneratePaymentLink`, `cancelCharge`, `fetchChargeStatus` (the truth the webhook is only a hint for), `parseWebhook` (the per-method sequences: Pix `PAYMENT_RECEIVED` only; boleto `CONFIRMED`→`RECEIVED`; card `CONFIRMED` then `RECEIVED` ~32 days later) and the provider-health read / webhook re-enable **as S01 of M25 observed them**. `startAutoBilling` / `revokeAutoBilling` throw a typed `NotSupported` (M29). **No duplicate provider objects (M26-S05 M8 rule).** Asaas does not enforce uniqueness on `externalReference` (two creates gave two customers and two charges in the sandbox), and only `POST /payments` honours an `Idempotency-Key` header, answering a repeat with 409 and an empty body (`docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md` A23, observed), so a retry after an ambiguous timeout or a crash between the call and storing `READY` could create a second payment or customer. Therefore `createCharge` sends the charge id as the `Idempotency-Key` and treats a 409 as "already received", then looks the charge up by `externalReference` (`GET /payments?externalReference=<chargeId>`, observed to filter) and returns the existing one; `ensureCustomer` looks the customer up by its `externalReference` before creating (the lookup is not atomic and `POST /customers` ignores the key, so two concurrent first calls for one tenant are serialized per tenant by the caller), and the HTTP client does **not** blindly retry a `POST` that may have reached the provider: it retries only connect-timeout / connection-refused (request never sent) and idempotent `GET`s; an ambiguous `POST` failure surfaces as a retryable error, and the next attempt starts with the lookup. **Status and event mapping (a table in the adapter and in `docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md`, unit-tested against recorded fixtures):** webhook events — `PAYMENT_CONFIRMED` / `PAYMENT_RECEIVED` → `PaymentConfirmed` (first of the two wins, the other is a no-op), `PAYMENT_OVERDUE` → `PaymentOverdue` (confirm-only), `PAYMENT_REFUNDED` → full refund, `PAYMENT_PARTIALLY_REFUNDED` → partial refund (no access change), `PAYMENT_REFUND_IN_PROGRESS` / `PAYMENT_REFUND_DENIED` → logged, no change, `PAYMENT_CHARGEBACK_REQUESTED` / `_DISPUTE` / `PAYMENT_AWAITING_CHARGEBACK_REVERSAL` → dispute opened (no access change, `billing_dispute_opened` log), the lost-dispute event → `PaymentChargeback` **only once verified**, `PAYMENT_CREATED` / `PAYMENT_UPDATED` / `PAYMENT_DELETED` / `PAYMENT_RESTORED` and **every unknown event type → ignored, still `200`**. `fetchChargeStatus` statuses (*the sandbox reached only `PENDING`, `RECEIVED`, the card `CONFIRMED` and `REFUNDED`; the rest of the list is from the documentation, `docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md` G16*): `PENDING` → `PENDING`; `CONFIRMED`, `RECEIVED`, `RECEIVED_IN_CASH` → `PAID` (the card `CONFIRMED` is access-restoring by design); `OVERDUE` → `OVERDUE`; `REFUNDED` → `REFUNDED`; `REFUND_REQUESTED` / `REFUND_IN_PROGRESS` → unchanged (`PAID`); `CHARGEBACK_REQUESTED` / `CHARGEBACK_DISPUTE` / `AWAITING_CHARGEBACK_REVERSAL` → `PAID` with a dispute flag; `DUNNING_REQUESTED` / `AWAITING_RISK_ANALYSIS` → unchanged; a payment returned with the `deleted` flag → `CANCELED`. **`Charge.method` source:** the charge is created `UNDEFINED`, so `method` stays null until the payer picks; it is filled from the payment's `billingType` on the paid fact (PIX / BOLETO / CREDIT_CARD) and, if Asaas still reports `UNDEFINED` (`docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md` G9), recorded as unknown and the settlement margin keys on `payment_return_seen_at` (M25-S01 check 7 observed which). The `receiptUrl` for `markPaid` is read from the payment's `transactionReceiptUrl` / `invoiceUrl` by `fetchChargeStatus` (`NormalizedChargeStatus.receiptUrl`). The provider-side "cannot cancel because it is already paid" error is raised as the typed `ChargeAlreadyPaidError` (M26-S05/S06; the contract suite asserts it). Asaas requires a `User-Agent` header on API calls (an empty one gets 400 `user_agent_not_informed`, observed, `docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md` A24): the HTTP client always sets one. The outbound HTTP client carries an **explicit retry config and per-attempt timeouts** (undici excludes `POST` and connect-timeout errors from its retry defaults; a shared `AbortSignal.timeout()` caps total time, not per attempt) and never logs secrets or the tax id. Registered with `useClass`; **every key is registered in every environment, so the app always boots; `resolve()` rejects a live key outside production and a `*_FAKE` / `*_SANDBOX` profile key in production** (the M25-S14 guard, on lookup only, never at startup). A live adapter instance that needs the live secrets is provided only when those secrets and `FEATURE_FLAG_BILLING_PROVIDER_LIVE` allow it, which is a configuration condition, not an exception. While `FEATURE_FLAG_BILLING_PROVIDER_LIVE` is `false` (the production default until S39, S01) the registry refuses live merchant keys and the production boot does not require the Asaas secrets; the adapter's live profile is only constructed when the flag is `true`. **Contract suite run:** the existing `billing-provider.contract.ts` runs against the Asaas **sandbox** (staging keys), asserting that the fake and Asaas agree on every behaviour the money loop depends on **that a sandbox can produce**. A GitHub runner has no public URL (it cannot receive webhooks), the sandbox has no overdue action and rejects a past due date (`docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md` § 5), the card `CONFIRMED` → `RECEIVED` ~32-day gap cannot occur in a nightly run, and a lost chargeback cannot be produced in the sandbox (M25-S01 deferred it). The story therefore ships a **case-by-case table** in `docs/TESTING_STRATEGY.md`, each case tagged:
- *sandbox-live* (API calls and `fetchChargeStatus` reads): create customer, create customer twice yields one, create charge, create charge twice yields one (the same `Idempotency-Key` answers 409, then the lookup by `externalReference`), link, regenerate link, cancel, cancel of a paid charge raises `ChargeAlreadyPaidError`, paid (via `POST /v3/sandbox/payment/{id}/confirm`, observed in M25-S01 check 3) read back as `PAID`, refund;
- *recorded-payload* (normaliser only, payloads captured from the sandbox / docs): the per-method event sequences, duplicate and out-of-order delivery, idempotent re-delivery, invalid token / signature, unknown event ignored;
- *fake-only*: overdue (unless the sandbox endpoint is captured), delayed card `RECEIVED`, chargeback lost, invalid location, provider 5xx.
Any divergence is fixed in the **fake** (and the corrected sequences in `docs/TESTING_STRATEGY.md`) or reported as a provider fact. **Nightly job:** a scheduled workflow `.github/workflows/nightly-billing-contract.yml` runs the sandbox-live and recorded-payload cases using the sandbox keys as repository secrets; it is **not a required check** on `main`, a failure opens an issue rather than blocking merges (`docs/CI_CD_PIPELINE.md` § Nightly billing provider contract job). **Workflow hygiene (this repository and its logs are public, `docs/GITHUB_WORKFLOWS_GUIDELINES.md` §7):** `permissions: contents: read` plus `issues: write` for the failure step only, `persist-credentials: false`, SHA-pinned actions like `weekly-jobs.yml`, `schedule` + `workflow_dispatch`; the issue step de-duplicates (it comments on the open "nightly billing contract failing" issue instead of opening a new one each night); the suite output never prints invoice URLs, customer names, tax ids or keys (the logger redaction AC below also covers the test output), and a failure issue carries the failing case names and a run link only. The sandbox account is **shared with staging**, so nightly payments also fire webhooks at the staging BFF webhook (A2: unknown customer / charge → `200`, no state change); the nightly customers carry a recognisable `externalReference` prefix. The sandbox key exists in two places (Secret Manager for staging, S01; the GitHub repository secret for the nightly job); the S36 secret-rotation runbook entry covers both copies. **Gate:** M25-S01 already **observed** in the sandbox (`docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md` § 2) `invoiceUrl` behaviour after cancel and after paid, the webhook payload fields including `externalReference`, the `externalReference` maximum length (100 characters) and the minimum charge per method (R$ 5,00); S37 **re-confirms** those results on the built adapter. Two items the sandbox could **not** show stay production gates and are not S37 sandbox checks: `invoiceUrl` behaviour after the due date (G1) and whether `notificationDisabled = true` silences paid receipts (G4); S37 also does not rediscover the results above (the recorded request / response fixtures are produced from them) and records any difference in the provider doc. What is genuinely **new** here and recorded in the provider doc: **the event that finalizes a lost chargeback** (A11, gate 5) — the sandbox cannot produce a lost dispute, so it is verified from Asaas's documentation / support or from the first real event, and until then the normalizer maps no chargeback event to `PAYMENT_REVERSED` (S04) and the S04 chargeback-suspension criterion stays carried open (the manual runbook path covers it, S36); Asaas's **callback-domain rule** for the `returnUrl` (S28; *unverified, own knowledge: the `successUrl` domain must match the account's registered site*); the sandbox payment-confirm endpoint and any force-overdue endpoint (*unverified*); and production-account behaviour, which S39 re-checks. Gate 4's follow-up stands: S12 sends Ikaro's own `ChargePaid` receipt; if Asaas still sends one the duplicate is recorded and the S12 copy adjusted. The webhook-queue re-enable through the API was in scope of M25-S01 check 5 and is re-confirmed on the real adapter, not treated as unknown; if the API cannot re-enable the webhook, the self-heal alert path (S08) and the runbook already cover it.

**PR sequence:** 2 PRs. PR1 (app): the adapter, its unit tests, the contract run, module wiring. PR2 (devops, `infra-app-mix-ok` not needed — `.github/workflows` only): the nightly workflow and the repository secrets (**manual step stated explicitly:** the operator adds `ASAAS_API_KEY_SANDBOX` and the other sandbox secrets in GitHub → Settings → Secrets before the first scheduled run).
**IAM / permissions:** GitHub repository secrets (sandbox only); no cloud IAM change.
**Live-verification check:** (1) trigger the workflow manually (`gh workflow run nightly-billing-contract.yml`) and confirm it completes green against the sandbox, with the suite's test names visible in the run log; (2) on staging with a `BRL_ASAAS_SANDBOX` subscription, create a real sandbox charge through S05's job and pay it with the sandbox's payment simulator. The staging BFF ingress is internal-only (R7), so Asaas **cannot** reach it: the webhook is verified by replaying a payload recorded from the sandbox (with the sandbox token) against the staging BFF route (`200`, `provider_events` row, charge `PAID` after the `fetchChargeStatus` re-read), and by the nightly recorded-payload cases; real Asaas → BFF reachability is proven by S39's production smoke charge, before signups are opened; (3) confirm that with `APP_ENV=staging` the app boots and `resolve()` of a live key throws `MerchantProfileKeyNotAllowedError`; (4) the ingress / `vpc_egress` check of `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_INFRA.md` § Billing configuration (planned), per environment: on staging the backend's outbound calls to the Asaas API leave through the configured egress (a real sandbox charge created proves it); the webhook-path reachability by the provider is S39's production check; (5) every open gate named in the Gate paragraph (`docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md`) has its observed result written into the provider doc. Pass = the five observed.

**Backend use case steps:** none (adapter).
**Backend HTTP surface:** none.
**New migration / i18n keys / env vars / feature flags:** none (env from S01).

**Files to create/modify:**
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/providers/asaas-billing.provider.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`), `asaas-http.client.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`), `asaas-event.normalizer.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`) (new)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/providers/asaas-billing.provider.contract.spec.ts` (new — runs the shared suite; tagged to run only when the sandbox env is present)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/providers/fake-billing.provider.ts` (+ spec) (modify — any divergence the sandbox run finds)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/providers/billing-provider.registry.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`) (modify — M25-S14 created it and said "M26 adds the Asaas keys": `BRL_ASAAS_SANDBOX` and the live key now resolve to `AsaasBillingProvider`, guard unchanged)
- `packages/architecture-check/architecture-policy.json` (modify — M25-S14 says "M26-S37 appends the Asaas adapter path to each" `externalSideEffectPorts` entry, so `transactional-io` covers the Asaas adapter)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/billing.module.ts` (modify — Asaas adapter registered with `useClass`)
- `.github/workflows/nightly-billing-contract.yml` (new)
- `docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md`, `docs/TESTING_STRATEGY.md`, `docs/CI_CD_PIPELINE.md`, `docs/GITHUB_WORKFLOWS_GUIDELINES.md` (modify — observed results; "planned" becomes built), `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` (modify only if the built Asaas adapter or port differs from § Provider port — the method list, including `returnUrl`, the account-health read and webhook-queue re-enable, is already there, M26-D10)

**Acceptance criteria — product:**
- [ ] Charges for a Brazilian tenant are created at, and payments confirmed by, the real payment provider; nothing in the product changes shape from what the fake produced.

**Acceptance criteria — technical:**
- Unit:
  - [ ] Each port method against recorded HTTP fixtures; the **full event and status mapping tables** (Pix / boleto / card sequences, refund, partial refund, dispute opened, `PAYMENT_CREATED/UPDATED/DELETED/RESTORED`, an unknown event type ignored with `200`, every `fetchChargeStatus` status, the `deleted` flag, `Charge.method` from `billingType`); retry and timeout behaviour (a `POST` that may have reached the provider is not blindly retried); no secret, tax id, invoice URL or customer name in logs
  - [ ] **No duplicate provider objects:** `createCharge` twice with one `externalReference` and `ensureCustomer` twice yield one provider object (lookup before create); `ensureCustomer` maps email + address (CEP) and updates an existing customer; amounts convert `MinorAmount` ↔ Asaas decimal value without rounding drift
  - [ ] The registry resolves `BRL_ASAAS_SANDBOX` and the live key to the Asaas adapter, refuses a live key while `FEATURE_FLAG_BILLING_PROVIDER_LIVE=false`, and `architecture-check` flags a provider call inside `txManager.run()` on the Asaas adapter
  - [ ] `ensureCustomer` sends `notificationDisabled = true`; `createCharge` sends explicit zero interest and fine (`interest: {value: 0}`, `fine: {value: 0}`, accepted in the sandbox) and no late-fee field, and an `externalReference` within the verified maximum length of 100 characters (`docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md` § 1, item 1; A5, A10), asserted on the recorded request fixtures
  - [ ] **PCI:** no card data touches Ikaro — no PAN, CVV or expiry field exists in any request / response DTO, form, log line or database column of the Billing context, and the only payment surface is the provider-hosted invoice page (`docs/TECH_STACK_DECISIONS.md` § 10: Ikaro never stores card data and never renders a card form); a unit test over the Billing DTO and entity schemas asserts no such field name
  - [ ] Chargeback: the normalizer maps the verified lost-dispute event to `PaymentChargeback`; if it could not be verified, S04's chargeback-suspension criterion is recorded as carried open
  - [ ] Config guard: `resolve()` rejects a live key outside production and fake / sandbox keys in production; the app boots in every environment
- Integration:
  - [ ] The contract suite passes on the **fake** on every PR, and its sandbox-live and recorded-payload cases pass on the **Asaas sandbox** in the nightly job; each case is tagged in the table in `docs/TESTING_STRATEGY.md` (fake-only cases are named, never silently skipped)
  - [ ] Nightly workflow: least-privilege `permissions`, `persist-credentials: false`, SHA-pinned actions, de-duplicated failure issue, no PII / URL / key in the log (workflow lint clean)
  - [ ] The sandbox contract run creates a customer with an alphanumeric CNPJ (`12ABC34501DE35`) and a charge for it, and records the result as launch gate G17 in `docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md`; a refusal is a stuck condition for the BR charge path, not a silent skip
- Tenant isolation: none — the adapter carries no tenant data beyond the provider customer reference
- E2E: none — live check above
- [ ] Checkov / Tfsec clean (workflow lint)
- [ ] Coverage ≥80% on changed code
- [ ] `tsc --noEmit` clean, lint clean

### M26-S38 — Operator price changes — exact, percentage and the announcement fan-out (UC-112)

**Agent:** `backend-ts`
**Complexity:** L
**Docs to load:** `docs/USE_CASES.md` UC-112 (all, A1–A8), `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` § Billing — Provider Webhooks and Internal API (the three price endpoints and both request / response shapes), `docs/DOMAIN_MODEL.md` § Aggregate: FeaturePrice (versions, `batch_id`, supersession), `docs/DATABASE_SCHEMA.md` § `billing.feature_prices`, `billing.feature_price_versions`, `docs/DOMAIN_EVENTS.md` § SubscriptionPriceChangeAnnounced, `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_BACKEND.md` § Billing (planned) and § Transactions
**Dependencies:** M25-S04 (`FeaturePrice` aggregate, seed, read side), M25-S06 (error-code plumbing pattern), M25-S15a (`PlatformAdminGuard` in shared, operator audit pattern), M25-S16 and M25-S20 (gating decorators and the route-completeness detector for the new internal routes), M26-S03 (`SubscriptionPriceChangeAnnounced` topic), M26-S05 (the end-to-end AC: the issue job reads the version at the period start), M26-S12 (the announcement email handler)
**Pattern:** Command use cases with a `dryRun` mode computing the same result as the real run; one transaction writing versions + per-tenant outbox events; `previewHash` guarding the apply.

**Description:**
**Last wave, may follow launch** — the launch prices are seeded in M25-S04, so this story is the operator's way to change them later without a release. `PUT /internal/billing/feature-prices` creates a price (feature × country × tier × cycle, first `amountMinor`, currency) or sets `selectable = false` (A2: existing subscribers keep renewing it); `POST /internal/billing/feature-prices/adjust?dryRun=` is the normal way to raise prices (`percent`, `roundToMinor` default one whole real, `effectiveFrom`, `reason`, optional `cycles` / `exclude`): it selects every price of the country in force today that is `selectable` or still used by a non-exempt item, rounds half-up, and — **in one transaction** — inserts one `feature_price_versions` row per price sharing a `batch_id`, supersedes any pending version (A5) and writes **one `SubscriptionPriceChangeAnnounced` outbox event per affected tenant** with only that tenant's changed features, new total and its resolved `recipientEmail` (S03); `POST …/{featureKey}/{countryCode}/{tier}/versions?dryRun=` schedules one exact price. A **dry run** returns every new price, the affected tenants (`TRIALING`/`ACTIVE`/`PAST_DUE` with an active or next-period scheduled item in that country, not `exempt`; suspended tenants are not notified, trial tenants are), sample tenant totals and, for a percentage run, a `previewHash` — saving and sending nothing. Guards: currency must match the country (A1); cycle other than `MONTHLY` rejected until M30 (A3); an increase with less than `PRICE_CHANGE_MIN_NOTICE_DAYS + CHARGE_ISSUE_LEAD_DAYS` (30 + 10 = 40) days notice → `422 BILLING_PRICE_NOTICE_TOO_SHORT` (a decrease needs none but still an explicit future `effectiveFrom`, A4); a price that would not change → `422 BILLING_PRICE_UNCHANGED` (A7); the active prices moved between dry run and apply → `409 BILLING_PRICE_BATCH_CHANGED` (the apply carries `expectedPreviewHash`, A8); no affected tenants → versions saved, no events (A6). `effectiveFrom` is a **service-period start date**: an invoice snapshots the new amount only when its period starts on or after it, and issued invoices are untouched (S05's line computation already reads the version at the period start). The change is audited on `feature_price_versions` (`created_by`, `reason`, `batch_id`), not `operator_audit`.

**Backend use case steps:**
1. **`CreateFeaturePriceUseCase`**, 2. **`AdjustFeaturePricesUseCase`** (dry run + apply), 3. **`ScheduleFeaturePriceVersionUseCase`** (dry run + apply).
**Backend HTTP surface:** new controller `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/controllers/internal-billing-prices-admin.controller.ts` — the three routes above (`InternalApiGuard` + `PlatformAdminGuard`, each also `@NoFeatureGate()` with an explicit `@SuspensionAccess(...)` as the M25 route-completeness detector requires — read M25-S16 / S20 for the value used by `/internal/*` operator routes).

**Concurrency primitive (decided here).** `previewHash` only detects that the prices changed **between** the dry run and the apply; it is not a lock — two applies that both read before either commits would both pass it. The apply therefore takes a **row lock on the affected `feature_prices` rows**: `featurePriceRepo.findByCountryForUpdate(countryCode, ids?)` selects the country's price rows `FOR UPDATE` in one batched query with a deterministic `ORDER BY id` (CLAUDE.md §7 race primitives), then re-computes the hash **after** the lock and compares it with `expectedPreviewHash`; the existing `UNIQUE (feature_price_id, effective_from) WHERE superseded_at IS NULL` on `feature_price_versions` (`docs/DATABASE_SCHEMA.md`) is the second line of defence against two pending versions for one date. The lock is catalogued in `docs/LOCK_CATALOG.md` (a new Billing row, id `B-P1`; it never nests with B-R1 — the price apply touches no subscription row) with its named concurrency test below. **Fan-out batching:** the affected tenants are selected through the existing subscription-item indexes in pages (keyset by `tenant_id`), and the per-tenant outbox rows are written in batches inside the same transaction; the dry run reports the count so the operator sees the size. The operator route is rare and bounded by the tenant count (low thousands, the milestone's scale assumption), so one transaction is kept for atomicity (a rollback leaves no events); if the count ever exceeds a configured ceiling (`PRICE_ANNOUNCE_MAX_TENANTS`, a code constant of 5000 beside the other defined-once Billing constants, not an env var) the apply is refused with a clear message rather than partially applied.

**Error codes (this story is the first thrower, so it owns the plumbing — M25-S06 only covers the three tax-id codes):** `BILLING_PRICE_NOTICE_TOO_SHORT` (default `422`), `BILLING_PRICE_UNCHANGED` (`422`), `BILLING_PRICE_BATCH_CHANGED` (`409`), plus the currency-mismatch and annual-cycle rejections (A1, A3) as `422 BILLING_PRICE_CURRENCY_MISMATCH` / `422 BILLING_CYCLE_NOT_AVAILABLE`; the statuses are the defaults of `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` and are confirmed at story discovery. Docs: `docs/ERROR_CATALOG.md` entries for `BILLING_PRICE_CURRENCY_MISMATCH` (`422`) and `BILLING_CYCLE_NOT_AVAILABLE` are added in the audit fix; `BILLING_CYCLE_NOT_AVAILABLE` here means an annual price-change request while annual cycles do not exist, which is a different meaning from the one slice H of the plan-change flow gives it, and the catalog defines both uses.

**Plano tab.** UC-112 requires "the Plano tab and the public prices show the current amount and the upcoming one". The Plano tab's `upcoming` per item is supplied by **S17** (`GET /v1/billing/plan`) and rendered by **S30**; S23 supplies it for the public prices. This story only produces the dated versions they read; its integration test asserts that after an apply the three reads (`GET /plan`, public prices, summary `upcomingPriceChanges`) carry the upcoming amount.
**New migration / i18n keys / env vars / feature flags:** none migration; i18n error keys in `packages/i18n/locales/{pt-BR,en}/errors.json` for the five codes above (both locales).

**Files to create/modify:**
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/application/use-cases/create-feature-price.use-case.ts`, `adjust-feature-prices.use-case.ts`, `schedule-feature-price-version.use-case.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`, `.integration.spec.ts` each) (new)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/domain/feature-price.aggregate.ts` (+ spec) (modify — `scheduleVersion`, `supersedePending`, rounding)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/application/ports/feature-price-repository.port.ts`, `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/repositories/typeorm-feature-price.repository.ts` and its in-memory double (+ specs) (modify — `findByCountryForUpdate`, the affected-tenants selector)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/http/billing-error.mapper.ts` (modify — the five price codes), `packages/types/src/error-codes.ts`, `packages/i18n/locales/pt-BR/errors.json`, `packages/i18n/locales/en/errors.json` (modify — the five codes in both locales), `apps/web/shared/lib/i18n/error-codes-exhaustiveness.spec.ts` (modify only if the spec needs the new codes)
- `docs/LOCK_CATALOG.md` (modify — the B-P1 row and its concurrency test name)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/infrastructure/controllers/internal-billing-prices-admin.controller.ts` (+ `.spec.ts`, `.integration.spec.ts`) (new), `apps/backend/http/billing/price-admin.http` (new)
- `apps/backend/src/contexts/billing/billing.module.ts` (modify)

**Acceptance criteria — product:**
- [ ] The operator can preview and apply a percentage or exact price change for a country; every affected manager gets exactly one consolidated email with the dated change; issued invoices and the current period never change; the public price page and the Plano tab show the upcoming amount.

**Acceptance criteria — technical:**
- Unit:
  - [ ] Rounding half-up to `roundToMinor`; notice rule for increase / decrease / (annual rejected); each of A1–A8; `previewHash` stability
- Integration:
  - [ ] Dry run saves and sends nothing (row and outbox counts unchanged); apply writes versions and N events in one transaction; a rollback leaves none
  - [ ] **Concurrency (B-P1, a test that fails without the lock):** two applies on the same country started together serialise on `findByCountryForUpdate`; the second re-computes its hash after the lock and is rejected with `409 BILLING_PRICE_BATCH_CHANGED`; exactly one batch of versions and one set of per-tenant events exist afterwards
  - [ ] An apply with more affected tenants than `PRICE_ANNOUNCE_MAX_TENANTS` is refused and writes nothing; below it, events are written in batches in one transaction
  - [ ] After an apply, `GET /v1/billing/plan` (S17), the public prices (S23) and the summary `upcomingPriceChanges` (S14) each carry the upcoming amount and date
  - [ ] End to end on the fake: S05 issues the next invoice at the old price before `effectiveFrom` and the new price for a period starting on or after it
  - [ ] Missing operator keys → `401`
- Tenant isolation: [ ] per-tenant events carry only that tenant's features and total; exempt and suspended tenants get none
- E2E: none — covered by integration
- [ ] Coverage ≥80% on changed code
- [ ] `tsc --noEmit` clean, lint clean

### M26-S39 — Launch readiness **[GATE]**

**Agent:** `devops + frontend-ts`
**Complexity:** M
**Docs to load:** `docs/discovery/tenant-billing/tenant-billing.md` (launch gates and open threads), `docs/RUNBOOKS.md` § Billing (planned), `docs/ENGINEERING_RULES_INFRA.md` § Billing configuration (planned), `infra/terraform/README.md` § New-resource PR-sequencing playbook, `docs/USE_CASES.md` UC-110, UC-111, UC-126, UC-127, `docs/DEPLOYMENT_STRATEGY.md`, `docs/TECH_STACK_DECISIONS.md` § 10. PAYMENT PROVIDERS
**Dependencies:** M26-S29 (the billing-identity form the address / tax id and NFS-e depend on), M26-S34 (the `TRIAL_SIGNUP_CTA` component the owner publishes, item 5), M26-S35 (E2E green), M26-S36 (alerts and runbooks live), M26-S37 (the real adapter and nightly job); transitively S24 / S33 (the signup flag it drills). S38 is deliberately not a dependency: price changes may follow launch.
**Pattern:** A checklist story: every item is a verifiable fact with a named check; nothing here is "write the code" — it is the go/no-go gate.

**Description:**
The go / no-go for charging real customers. The story is complete when each item below is **observed**, not asserted: **Order matters.** Production boots without the Asaas secrets until now, because `FEATURE_FLAG_BILLING_PROVIDER_LIVE` and `FEATURE_FLAG_SIGNUP_ENABLED` default to `false` in production (S01 / S24): production deploys were never blocked by the unpopulated secrets. This story turns them on in this order: items 1-3 and 4 (account, NFS-e, legal, Ikaro's tenant), then `FEATURE_FLAG_BILLING_PROVIDER_LIVE=true`, then the production smoke charge (item 8, which also proves real Asaas → BFF webhook reachability), and only then `FEATURE_FLAG_SIGNUP_ENABLED=true`.

**(1) Production provider account** — the Asaas production PJ account is approved (KYC done) and its production API key and webhook token are in Secret Manager (production values populated here, after S01 populated only sandbox); the webhook is configured at Asaas pointing at the production BFF route (with the `authToken`); the production `merchant_profile_key` is the live key; the contracted **fee table** and notification fees are confirmed in the account (PROVIDERS §4 item 3) and recorded. The Asaas facts S37 re-confirmed on the sandbox (`notificationDisabled` vs paid receipts, `externalReference` length, webhook payload fields, R$ 5 minimum, `invoiceUrl` after the due date) are re-checked against the **production** account and each is either closed in the provider doc or carried with a named owner; also verify whether `notificationDisabled = true` **silences the payer's NFS-e email**. An unverified lost-chargeback event keeps chargeback-driven `PAYMENT_REVERSED` **off**, and the manual `OPERATOR`-suspension path is the runbook's chargeback entry (S36). **(2) NFS-e** — provider-automatic NFS-e emission is configured once in the Asaas account (launch gate; no per-charge work), and the accountant has confirmed it satisfies the Brazilian duty. Prerequisites checked first (`docs/ASAAS_PROVIDER.md` A15 and its account-owner row, `docs/TECH_STACK_DECISIONS.md` § 10): a PJ account whose CNPJ has been active 6+ months (also what Pix Automático needs, M29), the municipal registration, the prefeitura's NFS-e homologation, the registered service, and the payer data Ikaro already collects (name, valid CPF / CNPJ, email, full address with CEP — S16 / S29 and the S05 address hold exist for this). The accountant also states the **NFS-e cancellation procedure** for a refund or a chargeback (recorded in the runbook), and the **fiscal retention period in years** for the charge history that survives a tenant purge — which must be recorded in `docs/DATA_RETENTION_AND_PRIVACY.md` **before the first real charge** (that doc marks it open until then). **(3) Legal** — the final Terms of Use and Privacy Policy texts and version are in place (replacing the S33 placeholders), the Terms state the cancellation / no-refund policy, the **40-day price-change notice** S38 enforces, and name **Asaas as a data processor** (owner / legal text — this story is the gate, M26 does not write it), and the production backend env `SIGNUP_TERMS_VERSION` (the single source; the web has no duplicate constant, S24 / S33) is set in the production Terraform to the version of the hosted text. **(4) Ikaro's own tenant** — create the platform-owner tenant with `scripts/provision-tenant.sh` using the `PLATFORM_OWNER_TENANT_SLUG` slug and the `exempt` flag; confirm it is `ACTIVE`, exempt, and cannot be un-exempted (D3: no production tenants exist before this point, so no production backfill is needed; the M25 backfill only ever ran on local / staging data). **(5) Hotsite CTA** — the owner's manager adds and publishes the `TRIAL_SIGNUP_CTA` module on Ikaro's hotsite. **(6) Alerts and schedulers live** — the six billing scheduler jobs, created **paused** in production by S05–S10, are enabled here, each alert policy exists in the production root (`module "monitoring"` is instantiated there — check, don't assume), and one throwaway production-safe dry run (a sandbox-keyed staging tenant going through the money loop with the production alert wiring mirrored in staging) has been observed. **(7) Rollback** — the rollback procedure (`docs/RUNBOOKS.md` § Rollback) is rehearsed for the Billing release, including the two levers that stop new exposure: **stop signups** — set `FEATURE_FLAG_SIGNUP_ENABLED=false` (the real kill switch, S24 / S33; unpublishing the `TRIAL_SIGNUP_CTA` module is **not** one, because `/ikaro-trial` stays reachable from existing links, UC-131 A3) and verify by loading the hotsite and `/ikaro-trial`, which must show the "cadastro indisponível" notice while the signup routes return `503 SIGNUP_CLOSED` and prices / slug availability stay readable — and **pause the six scheduler jobs** (`gcloud scheduler jobs pause ikaro-cron-billing-<name>` for each, then resume) so no charge is issued and no suspension applied while the cause is investigated. **(8) Production smoke charge** (`docs/TESTING_STRATEGY.md` § Testing the billing provider, "Live mode cannot be sandboxed") — with signups still closed and `FEATURE_FLAG_BILLING_PROVIDER_LIVE=true`, create a **dedicated non-exempt production test tenant** (never Ikaro's own, which is exempt and cannot be charged), issue one small real charge, pay it at Asaas, observe the **real webhook** reach the production BFF (`200`, `provider_events` row, charge `PAID`, the receipt email), check the NFS-e emission, then **refund** it (the safe refund order is in the runbook: this is a full refund of the first invoice of a `TRIALING` tenant, which suspends nothing) and cancel the NFS-e per the accountant's procedure; only then open signups (`FEATURE_FLAG_SIGNUP_ENABLED=true`). A "dispute opened" alert (S36) is wired in production. The M27 purge dependency is recorded: the first possible erasure is 90 days after the first suspension, so M27 must be scheduled before that date. No code is written here except small fixes the checklist exposes (each in its own tiny commit referencing this story).

**PR sequence:** per item, and **never one PR mixing `infra/terraform/**` with `apps/**`** (CI's `no-infra-app-mix` job): PR-A (devops) — production Terraform (scheduler jobs enabled via the `paused` switch from S05, `SIGNUP_TERMS_VERSION`, the two flags, monitoring instantiation if missing) following the playbook rows cited in S05–S08 / S36, then the explicit production Foundation apply; PR-B (frontend) — the final legal text pages (owner-supplied); `docs/**` changes ride with the PR they describe. Production secret population is a manual step (no PR).
**IAM / permissions:** production secret versions readable by the production runtime service accounts (already wired in S01); no new principal.

Pass = all eight observed; a failed or un-runnable item is a stuck condition, not a skip.

**Backend use case steps:** none.
**Backend HTTP surface:** none.
**New migration / i18n keys / env vars / feature flags:** none new.

Either way, `plan/M28-*.md` is drafted as a skeleton (goal, gate, known scope from § Reserved follow-ups, including the Stripe discovery and fake-update obligation carried from M25-S14), and its stories are written only if the gates are open; if they are not, the skeleton waits. M29 and M30 get the same one-line status. This does not block the launch.

(2) All Wave 2 stories edit the same BFF files and `packages/types/src/billing.dto.ts`, and all Wave 4 stories edit `packages/i18n/locales/*/web.json`: each wave is effectively sequential. (3) S18 now lists its Booking and access-answer files literally (no unenumerated consumers remain), but it edits M25-S13 files and the Booking window consumers, so it is not batchable with any story touching the Booking context or the access answer. (4) S24 and S26 both modify `platform-error.mapper.ts`: not batchable. (5) S01 and S24 both edit `infra/terraform/envs/{staging,prod}/main.tf` and the env-validation / env-contract files, and every devops half needs its Foundation apply and live check before the next infra story starts. (6) S36 and S37/S38 no longer share a glob; S36 lists the six job use cases it touches (which S05–S10 created) plus S04's `process-provider-webhook.use-case.ts` for the dispute log line. (7) **Behavioural overlap the file lists do not show:** S37 may modify `fake-billing.provider.ts` (a divergence the sandbox run finds) while S35's money loop relies on the fake — S35+S37 are listed as file-independent but should not run concurrently if S37's sandbox run is expected to change the fake. S36's live check and S37's staging live check both use staging, and S36 pauses the staging reconcile job, so they must not run at the same time. (8) S35 and S39 both depend on S29 / S34; S39 is last and runs alone. (9) Directory entries still present in some `Files` lists (S04's test-repository dir, S05/S06 `infra/terraform/envs/*/`, S12's use-case dirs, S18) are treated as overlapping everything beneath them and make those stories un-pairable; their owners replace them with explicit files (the earlier claim that none remained was wrong).

## Self-dry-run findings

Mechanical checks run over the draft (all clean after the fixes below): every story has the ten required schema fields (Agent, Complexity, Docs to load, Dependencies, Pattern, Description, Backend HTTP surface, Files to create/modify, product AC, technical AC); every `Dependencies:` edge points at an earlier-numbered M26 story or at an M25 story ID in S01–S31b (suffix ids: S15a/S15b, S18a/S18b, S21a/S21b, S31a/S31b) (a script cross-checks both) — a dependency is only ever backward; a *runtime or observation* reference to a later story (the S03 / S12 live checks using S13's endpoints, S06 reading the freeze flag S08 writes) is stated in that story as a precondition or a default value, never as an edge; every "modify" path either exists on disk or is created by an earlier M25 or M26 story (including bare filenames listed after a full path on the same line); no discovery-doc labels (candidate ids, decision numbers, cluster numbers, rule ids, the operations-map case ids) appear; every cited `docs/…` `§ heading` resolves against the real headings (loosely, for table-row labels in `docs/ANTI_PATTERNS.md`, as the repo already cites them); the mermaid graph is generated from the `Dependencies:` lines, not hand-drawn. **Post-audit re-check:** a script over the whole file (graph edges vs the M26 ids in each `Dependencies:` line, plus forward / cycle / cross-wave checks) found the original graph identical to the Dependencies lines, and the audit then added edges for dependencies the stories' bodies needed (S04→S05, S06→S19, S05→S37, S07→S37, S29/S34/S26→S35, S29/S34→S39, S27/S30→S32 with S19→S32 removed, S08→S24, and others other editors added); the script is re-run after every edit round.

Findings from the mechanical pass and from reading each story as `/story-discovery` would, **fixed in this draft**:
- **`BillingReminderDue` is a Command, not a domain event.** The promotion left the class-versus-command question open. Its idempotency key is a business key `(subscription, kind, daysOffset, chargeId)`, which is exactly what `shared.outbox.dedup_key` carries for a `Command` (precedent `BookingReminderDue`). A first version of S09 invented a reminder-deduplication column; settling the question in S03 removed it, so M26 adds no breaking migration (the one expand-only `platform.tenants` index is S08's, the one data seed is S11's).
- **The tenant-info port is not M26's.** An early S08 created the Billing → Platform read port; the M25 draft creates it (with its exact-path `permittedEdges`) for the provisioning window. S05 extends it with `isActive` and S08 **extends** it with `listTenantsCreatedBefore` (a new Platform read use case added to the same edge\'s `targetPaths`) so self-heal finds subscription-less tenants without a cross-context SQL join; no new edge direction.
- **Env vars.** M25-S07 owns `PLATFORM_OWNER_TENANT_SLUG`; S01 owns the Asaas secrets and the production-gating flag `FEATURE_FLAG_BILLING_PROVIDER_LIVE`; S24 declares and owns `FEATURE_FLAG_SIGNUP_ENABLED` (env schema, contract and behaviour); S39 turns both on in order (R7). Stripe secrets are M28.
- **Trigger-name convention.** The canonical docs name the triggers `billing-*` while the repo's trigger constants are `cron-…` strings that become literal topic names and are validated one-to-one against scheduler jobs (`CRON_LEAD_FORM_RETENTION_TRIGGER = 'cron-lead-form-retention'`). **Settled by the post-audit pass (no longer "verify at S05"):** the constants are `cron-billing-issue-charges`, `cron-billing-status-sweep`, `cron-billing-reminders`, `cron-billing-reconcile`, `cron-billing-self-heal`, `cron-billing-provider-events-purge` (scheduler job `ikaro-cron-billing-…`, manual route `POST /cron/billing-…`); the canonical docs' "Trigger name" column is corrected by the doc owner, and S05 adds the scheduler module's `paused` switch.
- **Wrong doc homes.** The route-scoped chrome-state rule lives in `docs/DASHBOARD_FRONTEND_ARCHITECTURE.md` (Staff/Manager Shell), not Frontend rules; the Notification section heading is "3. Notification Context"; the Staff/Manager Shell heading has no "§ 1" in its text; the shared notification-template builder is a concrete file, not a directory.
- **Files lists.** Several lines mixed "new" and "modify" files, and compound lines listed bare filenames after a path (the parser now resolves them). Split the mixed lines (S08–S10, S22, S24, S25), replaced directory entries by explicit files where the files are known (S04, S11, S24, S26, S33–S36, S39), and pointed S26 at the real `update-hotsite-content.use-case.ts`. **Correction (post-audit):** the earlier claim that no directory entries remained was false — a re-run of the Files parser found directory entries in S04, S05, S06, S12, S18, S36 and S39 (`infra/terraform/envs/{staging,prod}/`, use-case directories, test directories). The post-audit rule (M2) is: explicit `main.tf` paths and named files only, with no directory, glob or prose entries; S36 and S39 now comply, and the owners of the others replace theirs (the run-batch preview treats a directory entry as overlapping everything beneath it, so a remaining one only makes a story un-pairable, never unsafe).
- **Coupling check.** Terms and Privacy hosting stays in the frontend public-pages story (S33) and out of the signup backend story: it shares no write boundary with signup (static pages plus a version constant), and a spec asserts the web and backend constants match. The reminders job (S09) and the email handlers (S12) are separate because they sit on opposite sides of a published command.

**Design checks applied while drafting (recorded so story discovery does not re-derive them, not defects):** `ApplyChargeFactUseCase` is one use case with three entry points (M26-D1); provider I/O is never inside a transaction — the webhook's `fetchChargeStatus` re-read happens before it, the stale-charge cancel in the sweep, plan save and cancel happens after the local commit, and `transactional-io` is an acceptance criterion in S04, S05, S06, S07, S15, S19 and S20; every use case touching both aggregates takes the subscription row lock (B-R1) before the charge rows (M26-D2), with named concurrency tests (webhook × sweep, webhook × webhook, cancel × webhook, plan save × issue job, double reactivate, double signup); the chatbot tier mapping (S21) precedes the Plano tab (S30) as `docs/TENANTS_SETTINGS_SCHEMA.md` requires (the chatbot UC numbers were renumbered by the promotion, so S21's Docs-to-load says to find them by title); S25's resend identifies the tenant from the signup that just happened so it cannot become an email-enumeration oracle; S27 deliberately leaves `proxy.ts` alone and S32 owns the suspended-manager repoint.

Judgement findings carried as **explicit open items** (not guessed): the statuses and codes listed under Known open items (charge-not-payable, plan rejections, public signup unlisted statuses, non-owner `TRIAL_SIGNUP_CTA`); the signup Google-step mechanism, the cookie name, the per-Google rate-limit storage, the `/ikaro-trial` rewrite and CSP allowance (S24, S33); how the editor learns the owner tenant and the invitation-resend identity (S34, S25); how Booking learns `bookableUntil` (S18, default: the shared access answer); the banner mount point (S32); (the trigger-name convention is now settled, see above); the exact Wave 2 backend route prefixes (mirroring the nearest sibling controller). The `Files to create/modify` lists for Waves 3–5 are best-effort snapshots — `/story-discovery` Step 1 re-verifies each against the live repo.

Supplying-endpoint check (every frontend story): S27 reads `GET /v1/billing/summary` and `GET /v1/billing/charges` (S14); S28 reads `payment-link` / `payment-return` (S15) and the summary; S29 reads/writes identity (S16); S30 reads the plan catalog and preview (S17) and writes via S19; S31 writes via S20; S32 reads the summary (S14) and the access answer (M25-S13); S33 reads prices (S23), slug availability (S22), the signup session (S24) and writes signup/resend (S24, S25); S34 saves through the existing hotsite endpoint (contract S26) and learns the owner tenant through a backend read named at story discovery (a supplying read **is** an open item there, stated as one — not silently assumed). No other frontend read lacks a supplying endpoint.

Anti-pattern table cross-check (CLAUDE.md §8): adapters registered with `useClass` (S37, S13); no cross-context SQL join (S08 extends the M25 tenant-info port; S18 uses one Billing → Booking port; S12's handlers live in Notification and read only event data); BFF controllers have sibling `.types.ts` / `.schemas.ts` / `.mapper.ts`, public controllers are separate files (`billing.webhook.public.controller.ts`, `billing.public.controller.ts`, `signup.public.controller.ts`) and no JWT-gated write routes sit under `/public/`; no `--ba-*` in dashboard components (S27–S32) while the hotsite-tree public pages (S33, S34) may use it; error codes added with translations in both locales (M25-S06 for the existing codes; S26 states the new non-owner code, if any, must add both); entities have builders (S02); no new interface in web `api` paths that duplicates `@ikaro/types` (billing DTOs live in `packages/types/src/billing.dto.ts`).
